PART 1 — AUDIT (independent fact-check; auditor did not write the original)

Last audited: 30 October 2026. Research date for the original: 30 October 2026. The 3 November 2026 election had not yet been held at that date, so no 2026 result is reported or certified in this file, and any 2026 measure is marked Verify where certification is still outstanding.

Method. Every claim below was checked against a primary source only: Nebraska Revised Statutes and bill pages on nebraskalegislature.gov, the Nebraska Administrative Code / Secretary of State, the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission (minutes, votes and regulations), the Liquor Control Commission where it supports the Commission, DHHS, the Department of Agriculture, the Department of Revenue, the Attorney General's official opinions and filings, the Secretary of State for ballot measures and certified results, Nebraska district and Supreme Court records, the Governor's official actions, official tribal government sources, and official county, city and village sites and codes. News, law-firm blogs, trade press and AI summaries were not used to confirm anything. Where only a secondary source exists, the verdict is Unverified. Verdicts: Confirmed = primary source supports it as written; Corrected = wrong in a material detail and a primary-supported wording is given; Removed = wrong and no correct version found, or a proposal presented as law; Unverified = no primary source located to confirm it.

Federal baseline check (supplied, not re-audited). The original states the baseline correctly in substance: Schedule III for FDA-approved products and state-licensed medical marijuana only, from 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714); all other marijuana, including all adult-use, remains Schedule I; the broader 2024 proposal is not final. It also states the Nebraska application correctly and plainly: as of 30 Oct 2026 no Commission licensee was licensed and operating a sale, so Schedule III / 280E relief had no operating Nebraska medical sale to attach to; patient possession under Initiative 437 with no state-licensed source is marked Verify where the federal rule's text does not settle whether it counts as Schedule III medical activity or Schedule I possession; any non-medical possession remains Schedule I. 280E relief is therefore applied only to a state-licensed medical licensee, never to adult-use activity, which Nebraska does not license. Hemp dates, the DEA-lab delay to 31 Dec 2026, the appropriations rider through 11 Dec 2026, and the absence of a federal banking law are stated consistently with the baseline. No federal-baseline statement required correction, except that the $0 sales figure and the "no sale" inference are kept as Verify for a published total (see row 31).

Part 0 / Part 1Initiative 437: patient may possess up to 5 ouncesConfirmedNeb. Rev. Stat. §71-24,104Keep: "Allowable amount of cannabis means up to five ounces of cannabis" (§71-24,104(1)(a)), excluding the weight of other ingredients in topical/oral administrations, food, drink or other preparations (§71-24,104(1)(b)).
Part 1Initiative 437 has no qualifying-condition listConfirmed§71-24,104Keep. A "qualified patient" is defined only by a written recommendation: a practitioner judgment that benefits outweigh harms for alleviation of a medical condition, its symptoms, or side effects of its treatment.
Part 1Recommendation rules: practitioner types, 2-year validity, under-18 guardian permissionConfirmed§71-24,104(5)–(7)Keep. Practitioner = physician, osteopathic physician, physician assistant or nurse practitioner licensed under the Uniform Credentialing Act, or licensed in any state and practicing in compliance with it. Written recommendation is valid for 2 years or a shorter period specified by the practitioner. Under 18: recommendation plus written permission of a legal guardian/parent with health-care-decision authority.
Part 1Caregiver rulesConfirmed§71-24,104(4); §71-24,105(1)(b)Keep. Adult patient not under guardianship: caregiver 21+ designated in a signed affidavit. Minor/guardianship patient: legal guardian/parent with decision authority, or a person designated by them in a sworn affidavit. A health-care facility or home-health agency may serve if designated by sworn affidavit and it agrees in writing.
Part 1What patients/caregivers may lawfully do under Initiative 437Confirmed§71-24,105Keep: it is not an offense under state/local law for a qualified patient to "use, possess, and acquire an allowable amount of cannabis and cannabis accessories" for alleviation of a medical condition/symptoms/side effects, or for a caregiver to assist as above; that conduct "shall not be subject to the Uniform Controlled Substances Act" (§71-24,105(2)).
Part 1 / Part 11Home cultivation is allowed for patients/caregiversCorrected§71-24,105Home cultivation is not authorised by Initiative 437 or Title 238. §71-24,105 protects only use, possession and acquisition. Cultivation is licensed-establishment only.
Part 1 / Part 12No patient registration or ID card; proof is the written recommendationConfirmed§§71-24,103–105Keep as enacted law at 30 Oct 2026. Do not import the introduced LB 1235 committee-statement description of a confidential patient/caregiver registry — the LB 1235 Final Reading text contains neither a registry nor a qualifying-condition list.
Part 1 / Part 12Out-of-state patient reciprocity / visiting-patient rule in NebraskaCorrected§71-24,104No reciprocity, visiting-patient or out-of-state-card provision appears in §§71-24,104–105. The practitioner definition allowing a practitioner "licensed in any state" practicing in compliance with Nebraska credentialing law is not a patient-reciprocity rule. Operational acceptance at a (non-operating) dispensary is Verify — do not claim other states' cards are honoured.
Part 1 / Part 4Commission created by Initiative 438; membership 3–5, LCC members ex officio, Governor may appoint up to 2, 6-year termsConfirmed§71-24,109Keep. The Commission is created by Initiative Law 2024, No. 438, §4, for registration and regulation of persons that possess, manufacture, distribute, deliver and dispense cannabis for medical purposes. No separate incorporation date was found; it took effect with the 12 Dec 2024 proclamation.
Part 4 / Part 9Commission statutory deadlines: criteria by 1 July 2025, granting registrations to begin no later than 1 Oct 2025Corrected§71-24,111Deadlines are as stated, but the second deadline was missed: the first two cultivator licences were offered 7 Oct 2025 and issued 8 Oct 2025. Do not describe the programme as on-schedule.
Part 92024 petition certification: 437 = 89,962 valid signatures; 438 = 89,856; threshold 86,499ConfirmedSOS ballot certification, 13 Sept 2024Keep. Secretary of State certified the November general-election ballot on 13 Sept 2024, including #437 Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection and #438 Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation.
Part 9Certified 2024 results: 437: 637,126 for / 259,643 against = 71.05%; 438: 600,481 for / 291,867 against = 67.30%; SOS canvass pp. 76–77CorrectedLegislative Journal, 8 Jan 2025 (SOS certification reproduced)437: 637,126 / 896,769 = 71.05% — confirmed arithmetically. 438: 600,481 / 892,348 = 67.29% (67.2922%), not 67.30% — corrected to 67.29% (67.3% to one decimal) everywhere in the rewrite. The pinpoint "SOS canvass pp. 76–77" was not directly opened, so that pinpoint stays Verify; totals are confirmed via the Legislative Journal reproduction of the SOS certification. State Canvassing Board certification was 2 Dec 2024.
Part 9Effective dates of Initiatives 437 and 438: 12 Dec 2024 vs 10 Dec 2024Corrected§32-1414; Kuehn v. Pillen opinion12 December 2024 for both, on Governor Pillen's proclamation (§32-1414: a measure is in full force from the date of proclamation). 10 Dec 2024 was the date Kuehn v. Pillen was filed / a TRO was sought, not the effective date. The secondary FAQ giving 10 Dec as the proclamation date is wrong and is removed as an alternative effective date.
Part 1 / Part 91978 small-possession infraction lawConfirmed§28-416; LB 808 (1978)Keep in substance: LB 808 (1978) made possession of an ounce or less an infraction. Year only is verified; do not state a month/day for LB 808, and do not back-project the current $300 first-offense fine to 1978 (an earlier figure was $100).
Part 1Current non-patient possession penalties, cited as §28-416(13)Corrected§28-416 (as amended through Laws 2026, LB 795, eff. 18 July 2026)Current tier is §28-416(14), not (13): ≤1 oz (or any substance containing a quantifiable amount of Schedule I (c)(28) substances), except as in §28-1701: (a) 1st offense — infraction, citation, $300 fine, and assigned course under §29-433 if the judge determines it is in the defendant's best interest; (b) 2nd — Class IV misdemeanor, citation, $400 fine, may be imprisoned ≤5 days; (c) 3rd+ — Class IIIA misdemeanor, citation, $500 fine, imprisoned ≤7 days. >1 oz to 1 lb — §28-416(12), Class III misdemeanor. >1 lb — §28-416(13), Class IV felony. Possession remains unlawful even when the first offense is only an infraction.
Part 1Cultivation penalty stated as a single felony classCorrected§28-416(1)(a), (2)§28-416 contains no separate "cultivation" tier. Cultivation falls under manufacture/distribute/deliver/dispense/possess with intent (§28-416(1)(a)). Penalty under §28-416(2): (a) Class II felony if a Schedule I/II/III substance "which is an exceptionally hazardous drug"; (b) Class IIA felony for any other Schedule I/II/III substance; (c) Class IIIA felony for Schedule IV/V. Do not flatly call marijuana cultivation Class IIA until §28-401's "exceptionally hazardous drug" list is checked against marijuana — that check stays Verify.
Part 92020 Supreme Court ruling removing the earlier initiativeConfirmedNeb. Const. art. III, §2State ex rel. Wagner v. Evnen, No. S-20-623, 307 Neb. 142, 948 N.W.2d 244, filed 10 Sept 2020, writ of mandamus granted. Holding: the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Constitutional Amendment violated the single-subject rule — "by our count, it contains at least eight subjects" — combining a patient right to produce/use with entity rights to grow/sell and regulation in at least six areas of public life (logrolling); the Secretary was directed to withhold it from the Nov 2020 ballot. It was never voted on in 2020.

Evnen, Lancaster County District Court No. CI 24-3244, Judge Susan I. Strong. Bench trial 29, 31 Oct, 1, 4 Nov 2024; 1 Nov motion to block tabulating/publishing votes denied; Order and Judgment on Bench Trial 26 Nov 2024 rejecting the challenges — petitions legally sufficient (89,962 / 89,856 vs 86,499 required). Appeal: Kuehn v. Evnen, No. S-24-901, 322 Neb. 161, affirmed 4 Sept 2026: the district court did not err in not stripping the presumption of validity from all pages notarized by a notary based solely on improper notarization of one or more pages; invalid signatures proved were too few to drop either petition below threshold. Status at 30 Oct 2026: affirmed; signature challenge closed.
Part 9 / Part 1Litigation over 2024 initiatives — post-election case "upheld the laws against federal challenge"CorrectedNebraska courts, Kuehn v. PillenKuehn v. Pillen, Lancaster County District Court No. CI 24-4307. Complaint filed 10 Dec 2024; TRO/injunction to block the proclamation denied; proclamation signed 12 Dec 2024. District court (Strong, J.) dismissed the second amended complaint (16 June 2025) for lack of standing, without prejudice (hearing 20 May 2025; dismissal order filed 26 June 2025). Appeal: Kuehn v. Pillen, No. S-25-503, 322 Neb. 297, filed 11 Sept 2026, affirmed the dismissal. The court did not reach the federal-preemption or separation-of-powers merits. Any line saying the Supreme Court "upheld the laws against federal challenge" is corrected to "affirmed dismissal for lack of standing; preemption merits undecided." No separate Commission-regulation lawsuit was confirmed.
Part 10Nebraska and Oklahoma v. Colorado — denied 21 Mar 2016, presented as a win/loss on legalityCorrectedU.S. Supreme Court No. 22O144Motion for leave to file a bill of complaint filed 18 Dec 2014; 21 Mar 2016: "Motion for leave to file a bill of complaint DENIED. Justice Thomas with whom Justice Alito joins, dissenting from the denial." The denial was without majority explanation and decided no merits question. Correct wording: "denied leave to file, 21 March 2016; no merits holding."
Part 12No per se THC driving limitConfirmed§60-6,196(1)Keep. §60-6,196(1) makes it unlawful to operate/be in actual physical control while under the influence of alcoholic liquor or of any drug; the numeric thresholds (0.08 g/100 mL blood; 0.08 g/210 L breath) apply only to alcohol. There is no THC concentration threshold. Drug DUI is an impairment standard. This is not a "no DUI risk" statement — a qualified patient can still be prosecuted for driving while impaired by cannabis.
Part 12Expungement is petition-based, not automaticUnverifiedTo confirm: §29-3523 and §29-2264Keep flagged Verify until §§29-3523 and 29-2264 are opened on nebraskalegislature.gov and quoted. Direction in secondary literature is petition-based set-aside/sealing, with no general automatic expungement, but that is not adopted as primary-confirmed here.
Part 12No school-administration statute for medical cannabis (whole-code negative)Corrected§§71-24,103–105Scoped correction: "Initiative 437 contains no school-administration provision." A claim that no Nebraska statute anywhere permits school administration was not exhaustively verified (school code ch. 79 and Title 238 were not searched for this point) — keep it scoped, not as a whole-code negative.
Part 4Commission licence types: cultivator, product manufacturer, dispensary, transporterConfirmedTitle 238, ch. 1 (SOS emergency-extension file, 16 Apr 2026)Keep. Four types: dispensary §004, cultivator §005, product manufacturer §006, transporter §007. Vertical licensing is prohibited (§§005.01, 006.01).
Part 4Caps stated as cultivator 4, manufacturer 4, dispensary 12, transporter 12CorrectedTitle 238, ch. 1, §§004.11, 005.11, 006.11, 007.11Cultivator: max 4 statewide (§005.11, +1 in a following year only if demand is found insufficient). Manufacturer: max 4 statewide (§006.11, same +1 rule). Dispensary: max 1 per district-court judicial district (§004.11, +1 per district if demand insufficient) — "12" is derived from Nebraska having 12 judicial districts; the rule text itself does not state "12". Transporter: max 1 per judicial district (§007.11). Do not present "12" as a number written in the rule.
Part 4Commission licence fees: a dollar fee schedule ($5,000 application / $20,000 cultivator / $20,000 manufacturer / $15,000 dispensary / $13,000 seed-to-sale, etc.) as current lawCorrectedTitle 238, ch. 1, §§003.08–003.09; LB 1235 Final, §8Current Title 238 text sets no dollar fee: §003.08 says an application fee, if required, is non-refundable; §003.09 says licences are 1-year if a fee is required, otherwise 6-month (the basis for cultivator 6-month renewals in MCC minutes). LB 1235 §8 authorises the Commission by rule to set application/registration/renewal fees, none exceeding $50,000. Any dollar schedule is a proposed-amendments figure only — see Unverified list — and $50,000 is a cap, not the fee charged.
Part 4Cultivator licences: 2 offered 7 Oct 2025; 4 seated by 20 Mar 2026CorrectedMCC Minutes, 16 Mar 2026; MCC Minutes, 11 May 2026; MCC Minutes, 14 Sept 2026 (draft)First two cultivator licences offered 7 Oct 2025, issued 8 Oct 2025, to Patrick Thomas and Midwest Cultivators Group (Nancy Laughlin-Wagner), from 39 applications received 4–23 Sept 2025, with a >70/100 average-score threshold. The 4th cultivator, Meadowlark Medicinals LLC (application 10), was offered licensure 16 Mar 2026 (score above 70), after Blue Ember Ventures LLC was denied 2 Feb 2026; KRL Med LLC's licence was issued 6 Jan 2026. Four cultivators are confirmed as existing by the 11 May 2026 meeting ("the Commission now has four cultivators") and the 14 Sept minutes ("approved 4 cultivator licenses"). No MCC minute dated 20 Mar 2026 was located confirming all four "seated" on that date — 20 Mar is removed as an issuance/seating date throughout the rewrite.
Part 4Only MahāMotā (Raymond) had plants in the groundCorrectedMCC Minutes, 22 June 2026; MCC Minutes, 17 Aug 2026Separate the facts. (a) MahāMotā was the only cultivator to have passed inspection by 22 June 2026 — June 22 minutes: "the Commission was able to complete the inspection for Mahamota, and Mahamota passed"; KRL Med and Meadowlark requested postponement. 11 May minutes authorised cultivators to begin growing immediately upon authorised inspection approval. (b) August 17 minutes: MahāMotā "continues to move forward with cultivation operations"; Midwest had only taken possession of a new Gretna facility and was monitoring manufacturing/dispensary progress before buildout; KRL Med was still in Washington County conditional-use proceedings and had not yet had an MCC inspection; Meadowlark was in renewal. Corrected wording: "By June–August 2026, only MahāMotā had passed inspection and was proceeding with cultivation; the other three had not." The Raymond town attribution for MahāMotā's site is secondary-only in the minutes located — keep Raymond only where the original cites a secondary site report, flagged accordingly, not as an MCC-minute fact. "Plants in ground" as a phrase is not in the minutes.
Part 4 / Part 11Cultivator plant cap 1,250 flowering plants, cited as "draft §12.06"CorrectedTitle 238, ch. 1, §014.06(A)–(C)The cap is not draft-only and not §12.06. In the Title 238 text on file, §014.06 caps a cultivator at no more than 1,250 flowering plants at one time, whether indoor, outdoor, greenhouse, or any combination. Cite §014.06, not §12.06, unless a renumbered proposed-amendment text is produced (see Unverified list).
Part 4Product-manufacturer licences: 0 awarded; 10 applications by 14 Sep 2026; licensing held pending final fee rules expected early 2027ConfirmedMCC Minutes, 14 Sept 2026 (draft); MCC Minutes, 17 Aug 2026Keep with qualification: 0 manufacturer licences awarded through the 14 Sept meeting; 14 Sept minutes: "the Commission currently has ten manufacturer applications pending." Timeline: applications solicited 24 June–20 July 2026 (22 June motion), extended to 17 Aug (20 July), extended to 14 Sept (17 Aug, when only 3 had been received), then on 14 Sept the Commission voted to keep the 10 pending applications open through December 2026 rather than advancing them. "By 14 Sep" is a snapshot count, not a closed-window total. 14 Sept minutes: "the proposed regulations are expected to become permanent in early 2027" and existing applicants "will need to amend and resubmit"; 17 Aug minutes: fees cannot be collected until the applicable regulations are adopted and become effective, and applications would not be reviewed during the extended period.
Part 4Dispensary licences: 0 awarded; draft sale cap 5 g Total THC per 90 days, cited as "draft §11.09"CorrectedTitle 238, ch. 1, §§013.07–013.080 awarded — confirmed; no dispensary application period is recorded in the MCC minutes located. The sale rule is not draft-only and not §11.09. §013.07 provides: a dispensary may not sell/transfer more than a 30-day supply based on the written order's dosage rate; may not sell/transfer more than five (5) grams of Total THC to the same patient (or for the same patient) in a ninety (90) day period; may not sell within 30 days after filling a valid written order; and may not sell more than once for the same written order (§013.08, no refills). The measure is Total THC, not delta-9 THC only.
Part 11Title 238 adoption: emergency vs permanent; "21 August 2026 DRAFT" as the text quoted in Part 11CorrectedGovernor's Office, 1 July 2026; SOS emergency-extension file, 16 Apr 2026; MCC Minutes, 17 Aug and 14 Sept 2026Sequence: (1) emergency/temporary regulations, extended 16 Apr 2026; (2) Attorney General approval 30 June 2026, Governor approval of permanent regulations 1 July 2026 — regulations "will be filed with the secretary of state's office and will become law five days after receipt" (so a 6 July effective date is the 5-days-after-filing calculation, not a date stated in the release itself); 14 Sept minutes confirm "temporary regulations that have since become permanent." There is no 21 Aug 2026 draft in the MCC record located; the relevant date is 17 Aug 2026. On 17 Aug the Commission voted only to approve proposed regulations for purposes of proceeding with formal rulemaking — Chair Mueting expressly clarified this "was not a vote to make the proposed regulations effective." Remaining steps (publication, public comment/hearing, Commission adoption, AG review, Governor approval) were still ahead. 14 Sept minutes set the public hearing for 15 Oct 2026 (written comments to 11:59 p.m. 15 Oct) on proposed amendments "primarily concerning" fees, expected to become permanent in early 2027. The Aug 17 package is therefore a proposed amendment package to the already-permanent July 2026 regulations, not the permanent text itself, and it was still draft/proposed at 30 Oct 2026. Caveat: the SOS PDF on file is the emergency-extension file, not a distinct SOS "permanent Title 238" publication page — do not cite that file URL as proof of permanent publication without this caveat, and re-open the SOS codified Title 238 before republication. The 19 Oct 2026 meeting agenda lists only a "Public Hearing Update" — 19 Oct minutes were not located, so no post-hearing adoption can be confirmed.
Part 11Regulations narrowing Initiatives 437/438 — attribution to the initiativesCorrectedTitle 238, ch. 1, §§013.05–013.09, 014.06Yes, and the attribution must change everywhere: these narrow the initiatives and come from Commission regulation, not Initiatives 437/438: (a) dispensary sales only to patients with a valid, unexpired written recommendation meeting chapter requirements, plus a Recommending Health Care Practitioner Directory (§§013.05, 002.13/002.68 context); (b) the 5 g Total THC/90-day, 30-day-supply and no-refill limits (§§013.07–013.08) on top of the initiative's 5-ounce possession ceiling; (c) the closed product-form list and bans (§013.09), including raw flower and smoking/vaping, which the initiative text does not impose; (d) the 1,250-plant cultivator cap (§014.06) and 4/4/1-per-district licence caps.
Part 11 / Part 12Product forms: allowable vs banned, THC capsConfirmedTitle 238, ch. 1, §013.09 (text on file; SOS codified text to be re-opened before republication)Allowable: (i) oral tablets, capsules, tinctures; (ii) gels, oils, creams/topicals; (iii) suppositories; (iv) transdermal patches; (v) nasal sprays; (vi) liquids/oils for nebulizer/inhaler. Unallowable: (i) raw plant material; (ii) smoking/combustion/vaping products; (iii) artificial/natural flavouring or colouring; (iv) cannabis baked/mixed/infused food or drink; (v) synthetic cannabinoid substances; (vi) >60% Total THC or >40 mg/dose; (vii) food-confusing packaging; (viii) human/animal/leaf shapes; (ix) tobacco/alcohol/other intoxicant; (x) unregistered product. Note: this is regulation, not initiative text.
Part 1 / Part 2 / Part 4LB 316: indefinitely postponed 17 Apr 2026, not lawConfirmedNebraska Legislature — LB 316 / LB 933 detail, Journal p. 1713Keep the status, correct the mechanism: 17 Apr 2026 was the last day of session, and Journal p. 1713 is the blanket motion indefinitely postponing all bills not otherwise disposed of — not a standalone floor vote on LB 316 alone. LB 316 had previously been passed over 30 May 2025 and carried over to 2026; it received no 2026 floor passage. LB 316 is a hemp bill (Kauth, 109th: "Prohibit conduct relating to hemp other than cannabidiol products…"), not a medical-cannabis programme bill — do not describe it as amending Initiatives 437/438, and its proposed caps must not be presented as current limits.
Part 1 / Part 2 / Part 4LB 1235: enacted, Final Reading 46-2 on 1 Apr 2026, operative 8 Apr 2026CorrectedLB 1235 Final Reading text; §71-24,109Vote/date/approval confirmed: Final Reading 1 Apr 2026, 46-2 (with emergency clause; 1 absent/not voting, shown as 46-2*-1), presented to the Governor 1 Apr, approved 7 Apr 2026. Operative-date correction: Final Reading text §10 makes §§1–4, 11 operative three calendar months after adjournment (the liquor provisions), while "the other sections … become operative on their effective date" (§13 emergency clause: on passage and approval). The statute page for §71-24,109 lists "Operative Date: April 8, 2026" — the day after approval — which is the date to cite for the MCC provisions, not 7 Apr itself. Scope correction: the enacted LB 1235 is narrow — MCC member salaries of $12,500/year (§6), the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission Cash Fund (§7), fee-setting by rule capped at $50,000 (§8), and FBI fingerprint background checks via State Patrol (§9), plus liquor provisions. It does not enact the introduced bill's patient/caregiver registry, qualifying-condition list, or broad licensure framework described in the Feb 2026 committee statement — that broader text was struck by amendment (AM2178) before passage. Do not attribute a registry, condition list, or practitioner directory created by LB 1235 to the enacted law; the directory in Title 238 comes from Commission regulation.
Part 1 / Part 2Other 2026 bills affecting the programmeConfirmedNebraska Legislature bill status listsLB 933 (Cavanaugh, J.) — "Provide immunity for health care practitioners under the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act" — indefinitely postponed 17 Apr 2026 (same blanket end-of-session motion, Journal p. 1713), after being passed over 7 Apr 2026; not law. Also indefinitely postponed: LB 934 (election of MCC members). No other 2026 LB enacting programme changes was located; LB 1 (2025, technical correction to Initiatives 437/438) predates 2026.
Part 2Medical cannabis subject to an enacted 4% or 5.5% sales tax / exciseRemovedLB 1235 FinalLB 677 (2025) would have imposed a 4% special sales tax; it stalled after a failed cloture motion (23-22, 33 needed) — proposed only, never enacted. LB 1235 as introduced contained sales-tax language, but the Final Reading text enacted 1 Apr 2026 contains no sales-tax provision and no §77-4303 amendment. Any "4% medical sales tax" or "5.5% medical sales tax" stated as current Nebraska law is Removed; both proposals must be labelled "proposed" wherever mentioned. LB 677 committee amendment would have raised it to 5.5% + allowed a 2% municipal add-on — also failed, not law. LB 1235 as introduced proposed placing dispensary sales under the normal sales-tax structure with revenue to school property-tax relief — dropped before Final Reading, not law.
Part 2General sales tax / local sales taxes as applied to medical cannabis; hemp product taxUnverifiedDept. of Revenue — local sales/use tax rates; §77-2704.09General Nebraska state sales/use tax rate is 5.5%, with local-option taxes varying by jurisdiction — that is the general rate, not a cannabis rate. Whether a future licensed dispensary sale would be taxable tangible personal property at 5.5% + local, or exempt, is not resolved by any DOR cannabis guidance located: §77-2704.09 exempts drugs "sold for a patient's use under a prescription," and medical cannabis is obtained on a practitioner recommendation, not a prescription — do not claim the exemption applies. No hemp-specific product tax or excise was located.
Part 2Illicit-drug (marijuana) tax applied to lawful medical possessionCorrected§77-4303§77-4303: $100 per ounce (or portion) of marijuana; $150 per gram for controlled substances sold by weight; $500 per 50 dosage units otherwise. It is a dealer tax (DOR guide: dealer = illegal acquisition/possession of ≥6 oz marijuana, etc.). §77-4303(3) excludes "a person registered or otherwise lawfully in possession… pursuant to Chapter 28, article 4." LB 1235 Final did not amend this section, so do not claim lawful medical patients/operators were removed from it by LB 1235; its application to Initiative 437/438 possessors is Unverified and must be flagged, not assumed.
Part 2Nebraska conformity / decoupling from IRS 280ECorrected§77-2714; §77-2716No Nebraska §280E decoupling statute or §77-2716 adjustment adding back §280E-disallowed expenses was located. Correct wording: "Nebraska has no located §280E decoupling or add-back provision; its income tax starts from the federal figure, so a federal §280E change flows through unless DOR provides otherwise — no DOR guidance on Schedule III medical licensees was located, so entity-level treatment is Verify." Per the federal baseline, any §280E relief reaches only Schedule III licensees (FDA-approved products and state-licensed medical marijuana); adult-use activity, which Nebraska does not license, remains fully subject to §280E.
Part 7NDA state hemp programme ended 31 Dec 2024; Nebraska is NOT a USDA-approved state-plan state; production licensing is USDA-direct from 1 Jan 2025ConfirmedNDA — Hemp; USDA AMS state/tribal plan list, updated 17 Feb 2026; §2-505Keep. LB 262 (2024, approved 16 Apr 2024; hemp sections operative 1 Jan 2025) transferred hemp-cultivation licensure/regulation to USDA and terminated the state Hemp Commission/Promotion Fund. NDA's own 2024 summary: "The Nebraska Department of Agriculture Hemp Program will cease to exist beginning January 1, 2025." AMS list lists Nebraska as "USDA Hemp Producer License" — not an Approved Plan. §2-505(1) now allows cultivation only by a USDA-licensed producer or a state-program-licensed producer under a USDA-approved tribal programme. Nebraska still regulates hemp transportation (§2-515).
Part 7Under USDA there is one producer licence type; no separate processor/handler/broker licenceConfirmed7 CFR 990.21Keep for production, with nuance: USDA issues a single Hemp Producer Licence under 7 CFR part 990, subpart C (rolling applications, HeMP platform), valid until 31 Dec of the year three years after the year of issue (§990.21(a)(6)). LB 262 repealed the old NDA licensing sections (§§2-502, 2-504, 2-506–2-514, 2-516, 2-517, 2-519); no successor Nebraska processor-handler or broker licence was created. A USDA producer licence authorises production, not a standalone Nebraska processing/handling licence — do not present it as a processor licence.
Part 7USDA producer-licence fee (dollar figure)Unverified7 CFR 990.217 CFR 990.21 sets out application contents (including a criminal-history report dated within 60 days) and the 3-year term but states no application fee, and the AMS programme page lists no fee schedule. Do not state a dollar fee, or "$0", as USDA-confirmed without an AMS fee statement; the FBI history-check cost is a separate third-party cost. The old NDA fee schedule died with the programme.
Part 7Hemp definition and THC measurement under Nebraska lawConfirmed§2-503(3)Keep: hemp = Cannabis sativa L. and any part, "including the viable seeds… and all derivatives, extracts, cannabinoids, isomers, acids, salts, and salts of isomers, whether growing or not, with a delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol concentration of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis." It "shall be considered an agricultural commodity" and "shall not be considered a controlled substance under the Uniform Controlled Substances Act." §28-401(14)(c): "Marijuana does not include hemp." There is no Nebraska total-THC (delta-9 + THCA) definition in §2-503 — the state test is delta-9 only (federal total-THC change after 11 Dec 2026 is per the supplied federal baseline, not re-audited here).
Part 7Delta-8 / converted or synthetic cannabinoids are banned by Nebraska statuteCorrected§2-503; §28-405, Schedule I(c)(14)No enacted ban. Two statutes point in opposite directions and no Nebraska court holding resolving the conflict was located: §2-503(3) covers "isomers" within the ≤0.3% delta-9 definition and says hemp is not a controlled substance, while §28-405, Schedule I(c)(14) lists "Tetrahydrocannabinols, including… synthetic equivalents…, derivatives, and their isomers with similar chemical structure and pharmacological activity… regardless of the numerical designation of atomic positions." The AG's position in its releases is that purely synthetic/converted THC products are Schedule I and unlawful, but its filed cases plead Consumer Protection Act / Uniform Deceptive Trade Practices Act and Nebraska Pure Food Act violations, mislabelling and over-limit delta-9, not a standalone delta-8 possession conviction. Correct wording: "No Nebraska statute in force at 30 Oct 2026 names or bans delta-8; its legality is unresolved in statute and is the subject of AG civil enforcement."
Part 7Hemp-derived delta-9 productsConfirmed§2-503Keep within the limit: hemp-derived delta-9 is lawful only insofar as the product/plant meets §2-503: delta-9 ≤0.3% on a dry-weight basis. Anything over that limit is marijuana under §28-401, which is how the AG's releases frame their test purchases.
Part 7THCA — standalone Nebraska rule banning or allowing itUnverifiedAG — delta-8 enforcement announcementNo Nebraska statute or regulation located names THCA. The AG's Omaha release lists "Kushy Exotic THCA Flower" among products in an investigation where stores had over-limit delta-9 and mislabelled products, and says evidence of marijuana sale would be referred for criminal consideration — an enforcement allegation tied to delta-9/mislabelling, not a statute banning THCA per se. Do not state THCA flower is legal or illegal in Nebraska on primary sources alone.
Part 7Minimum purchase age, hemp testing/labelling rules, THC-beverage rules, or other Nebraska law restricting hemp-derived THC productsCorrected§2-503None enacted. No Nebraska statute or regulation in force at 30 Oct 2026 sets a minimum age for hemp-derived THC products, a hemp-retailer licence, hemp-product testing or labelling standards, a THC-beverage licence, or a state mg/serving or per-container cap. The only enacted levers the AG invokes are the general Consumer Protection Act, Uniform Deceptive Trade Practices Act and Nebraska Pure Food Act, plus the delta-9 limit itself. Any "21+", testing, labelling or beverage-cap claim for Nebraska is a retailer practice or a failed proposal (LB 316), not law. Production-side testing (pre-harvest sampling, DEA-lab enforcement delayed to 31 Dec 2026) is USDA programme law under 7 CFR 990, not a Nebraska retail rule.
Part 7 / Part 1AG hemp-retailer lawsuits: 15 in the AG's own release (12 settled, 3 litigated); 16 in a later secondary update — timing difference, Verify current countCorrectedAG release, 1 Aug 2025 — Savage Enterprises suit; AG archived newsResolution: 15 → 16 is a progression, not a contradiction. The Omaha-expansion release states: "To date, the Attorney General's Office has filed 15 lawsuits… 12 out of 15… have been settled. The remaining three are being actively litigated," alongside cease-and-desist letters to 104 Omaha retail locations / 35 companies — that is a dated snapshot, not a 30 Oct 2026 total. The 1 Aug 2025 AG release (Savage Enterprises suit) states "filed 16 lawsuits" and "sent 225 cease and desist letters." The 29 Sept 2025 AG release states "filed 16 lawsuits… 24 companies have signed the Assurance of Voluntary Compliance, and 12 of the 16 lawsuits have been settled," with cease-and-desist letters to companies owning ~300 stores. Current AG figure to use at 30 Oct 2026, unless a later AG release is found: 16 filed, 12 settled, 24 Assurances of Voluntary Compliance; 4 filed cases not described as settled (do not carry forward "3 litigated" — that arithmetic belonged to the 15-case snapshot). No AG release after 29 Sept 2025 was located in this check.
Part 7Case names, numbers and outcomes for all 16 AG hemp casesUnverifiedAG archived newsNo AG-published consolidated docket list, case-number table or outcome table was located. Only Savage Enterprises (Wyoming corporation, California-based; Consumer Protection Act / Uniform Deceptive Trade Practices Act; outcome not stated in the release) is named in an opened AG release. Earlier settlement names circulating in secondary coverage (e.g. Hall County / Saline County filings) were NOT confirmed from an AG release or court record and must not be reproduced as primary-confirmed. Confirm the full list from AG releases + Nebraska district-court (JUSTICE) records, county by county. Personal phone numbers or emails for any individual applicant/holder are not reproduced in this audit or rewrite — names are kept with a link to the official source instead.

Licensed: 1,651.05 outdoor acres + 92,066 sq ft indoor. Planted: 1,284.07 acres + 9,318 sq ft. Harvested: 1,275.07 acres + 9,102 sq ft. 15 of the 25 cultivators planted. 0 lots destroyed for non-compliance. These are licensed/planted/harvested counts — never present the 25 as "operating businesses" or the licensed acreage as planted acreage.

Part 3Counties/cities/villages have an express local opt-out / ban power over state-licensed medical cannabis under Initiatives 437/438 or later lawUnverified§71-24,111; Title 238, ch. 1, §§02.20, 08.04Do not state a local ban/opt-out power. What is primary-supported is narrower: licensees must comply with applicable local requirements, and cultivator signage must comply with county/local ordinances (§12.05 in the original numbering; see §014 context in the text on file). §02.20 defines "local government" as village, town, city or county; §08.04 sanctions non-compliance with "all applicable federal, state, or local government requirements." Any broader local veto is Verify.

PART 2 — REWRITTEN REPORT (corrections applied in place)

What follows is the complete original report, Parts 0–12 plus Research Notes and Appendix, with the audit corrections above applied. Where the original flagged an item Verify/Unavailable and the audit resolved it, the flag is removed; where it remains unresolved, the flag is kept.

Nebraska Cannabis & Hemp Research — Audited, Parts 0–12

Last audited: 30 October 2026. This is the auditor's corrected rewrite. Part 1 below is the audit table; the full Parts 0–12 report follows it with corrections applied in place. No section or detail from the original has been dropped; only what the audit changed has been changed.

Run 1: Parts 0–6. Run 2: Parts 7–12. Research date and source-access date for every fact: 30 October 2026, unless a fact itself states another date. The 3 November 2026 election had not yet been held on the research date, so no 2026 result is reported in this file.

How to read this file. Official government sources were used wherever possible and pages were opened directly; where a page could not be opened, or a point rests on secondary reporting rather than the official text itself, it is flagged Verify or Unavailable and the limitation is stated. No proposed bill, proposed regulation, enjoined action or pending lawsuit outcome is presented as current law. Nebraska has a one-chamber (unicameral) Legislature, so bill progress is reported by stage, not by chamber. Important text warning for Part 11: Part 11 quotations of Title 238, Chapter 1 are from the Title 238 text on file (permanent regulations approved 1 July 2026; the SOS file on file is the emergency-extension file, not a distinct permanent-publication page — re-check them against the Secretary of State's published Title 238 before republication, see Audit rows on Title 238). The 17 August 2026 package is proposed amendments (hearing 15 Oct 2026, expected permanent early 2027), not the permanent text.

Federal baseline (supplied, fixed context — not re-researched)

State-licensed medical marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714); adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I; the broader 2024 rescheduling proposal is not final. IRS 280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity; adult-use remains fully subject to 280E. Hemp: PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026, but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026; after 11 December 2026 the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply. USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026. The federal medical marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026. There is no federal cannabis banking law.

Nebraska application, stated plainly: as of 30 Oct 2026 no Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission licensee is licensed and operating a sale, so Schedule III / 280E relief has no operating Nebraska medical business to attach to yet. Patient possession under Initiative 437 with no state-licensed source is marked Verify where the federal rule's text does not settle whether it counts as Schedule III medical activity or Schedule I possession. Any non-medical possession remains Schedule I federally.

Parts 0–1 — Headlines and Laws

NEBRASKA — Cannabis and Hemp Research, RUN 1, PARTS 0–1 ONLY

Scope note: This file covers Part 0 (Headlines) and Part 1 (Laws) only, as commissioned. Parts 2–6 and 7–12 are not covered here except by brief cross-reference. Every fact below carries a source link and the access date 2026-10-30, as instructed. Where a page could not be fully opened or a point rests on secondary reporting rather than the official text itself, it is flagged Verify or Unavailable and the limitation is stated. No proposed bill, proposed regulation, enjoined action, or pending lawsuit outcome is presented as current law.

Fixed federal baseline (supplied, not re-researched): state-licensed medical marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714); adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I; IRS 280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity and remains fully applicable to adult-use; hemp PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026 but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026, after which the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply; USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026; the federal medical marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026; there is no federal cannabis banking law. Nothing found in this run contradicts that baseline on its face — but see the federal-position discussion in §1.1 and §1.5, where the interaction with Nebraska's not-yet-operational sales system is flagged Verify where federal text does not settle it.

Nebraska structure note: Nebraska has a one-chamber (unicameral) Legislature. Bill progress below is therefore reported by stage (committee / General File / Select File / Final Reading / Governor), not by chamber. Source: task instruction and the Legislature's own bill-history pages cited below, accessed 2026-10-30.

PART 0 — HEADLINES

0.1 Medical programme exists in law but is NOT operational for sales as of 30 Oct 2026

  • Nebraska voters approved two companion statutory initiatives on 5 November 2024: Initiative 437 (Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act) and Initiative 438 (Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act). Source: Nebraska Secretary of State 2024 ballot pamphlet text, https://sos.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/elections/2024/2024%20Ballot%20Measures%20Pamphlet.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Initiative 437 passed with about 71% and Initiative 438 with about 67% of the vote. Precise certified counts reported secondarily are 637,126 for / 259,643 against for 437 and 600,481 for / 291,867 against for 438 — Verify the exact certified canvass against the Secretary of State's certified results page, which was not directly opened in this run. Source (secondary, for the counts): https://kindgreenbuds.com/news/nebraskasupremecourt-unanimously-upholds-voter-approved-medical-marijuana-laws/ — accessed 2026-10-30. The 71% / 67% rounded figures are corroborated by multiple secondary reports of the Secretary of State's unofficial/certified results, e.g. https://www.cannabisbusinesstimes.com/top-stories/news/15707585/nebraska-voters-pass-medical-cannabis-legalization-measures-litigation-remains-ongoing — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Operating-licensee answer (the central question): NO business is operating a medical cannabis sale to patients in Nebraska as of 30 Oct 2026. No dispensary is licensed, no product manufacturer is licensed, and no lawful patient sale has occurred. Four cultivators are licensed (see below), but only one — MahāMotā Cultivation Company (Patrick Thomas, Raymond) — had plants in the ground, and with no licensed manufacturer to process and no licensed dispensary to sell, there is no completed supply chain. Sources: "No patient can legally buy medical cannabis in Nebraska today. No dispensary or manufacturer is licensed" (secondary summary of Sept 2026 position), https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ne_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-10-30; "no manufacturers or dispensaries permitted yet – and no patients registered" (15 Apr 2026), https://mjbizdaily.com/news/first-nebraska-medical-cannabis-harvest-could-come-by-fall-but-market-launch-uncertain/615467/ — accessed 2026-10-30; "As of September 13, 2026, state-licensed medical marijuana dispensaries have not yet opened" (secondary), https://themarijuanaherald.com/marijuana-laws-by-state/nebraska/ — accessed 2026-10-30. The absence of an official Commission licensee list was not directly verified on mcc.nebraska.gov in this run — Verify against the Commission's current list before republication (see Verify list at end of Part 1).

0.2 Cultivator conflict resolved: 2 in October 2025, 4 by March 2026

  • The "2 vs 4" conflict is a timing difference, not a contradiction. On 7 October 2025 the Commission offered the first two of up to four cultivator licences, to Nancy Laughlin-Wagner of Omaha (Midwest Cultivators Group LLC) and Patrick Thomas of Raymond, from 39 applications received 4–23 September 2025, with a >70/100 average-score threshold. Source (secondary reporting of the Commission meeting): https://www.omahadailyrecord.com/content/nebraska’s-first-medical-cannabis-cultivators-offered-licenses-legal-threats-loom — accessed 2026-10-30; also https://news.crbmonitor.com/2025/10/nebraska-awards-the-first-two-cultivator-licenses/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • By 20 March 2026 all four cultivator licences were seated. The four, named in a joint 15 October 2026 cultivator letter, are: Dustin Krajewski, Meadowlark Medicinals LLC; Kent Rogert, KRL Med LLC; Pat Thomas, MahāMotā Cultivation Co.; and Robert Wagner, Midwest Cultivators Group. Source: https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884965/us-ne-nebraska-s-four-licensed-cultivators-push-commission-on-testing-fees-and-physician-protections/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Corroboration that "all four cultivators have been licensed" by April 2026: https://couriernebraska.com/news/nebraska-medical-cannabis-commission-moving-forward-with-application-licensing-fees/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • First planting: the Commission voted to ratify the successful inspection of MahāMotā Cultivation Company in Raymond, clearing the first legal plants; a later report dates MahāMotā as "the only one of four licensed growers with plants currently in the ground" and its harvest "set for harvest within seven weeks" of an October 2026 meeting. Sources: https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30; https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884587/nebraska-cannabis-cultivators-may-wait-two-years-for-testing-guidelines/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify the exact planting/inspection date against Commission minutes (April 2026 meeting), which were not directly opened.

0.3 Dispensary cap: 12, one per judicial district; 0 awarded

  • Commission regulations cap dispensaries at 12, one per Nebraska District Court judicial district, and cap cultivators at 4 and product manufacturers at 4 (transporters up to 12 under the earlier regulation text; a later proposal would replace a standalone transporter licence with a manufacturer transporter certification — Verify against the current Title 238 text). Sources: regulation text snippet, Title 238 NAC Ch. 1, "No more than four (4) cultivator licenses shall be issued in the state," https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/Revised%20-%20238%20NAC%201%20DRAFT.8.21.final.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30; secondary summary of the 12-dispensary cap, https://mjbizdaily.com/news/nebraska-moves-to-ban-cannabis-flowers-and-edibles-limit-licenses/406659/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • 0 dispensary licences have been awarded as of 30 Oct 2026. The Commission has said it will not license manufacturers or dispensaries until the fee regulations are authorised. Source: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/nebraska-medical-marijuana-rule-updates-delayed-until-2027-as-10-manufacturer-applications-are-submitted/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

0.4 Regulations timeline

  • Emergency regulations: first emergency regulations took effect in summer 2025 (approved ahead of the 1 July 2025 statutory criteria deadline; extended in 2026 — the Secretary of State's emergency-regulations file is headed "MCC Extension Emergency Regs 4.16.26 signed"). Source: https://sos.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/regulations/Emergency%20Regs/MCC%20Extension%20Emergency%20Regs%204.16.26%20signed.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30 (file located via search; full text not extracted in this run — Verify).
  • Permanent regulations: Attorney General Mike Hilgers approved the permanent regulations 30 June 2026 and Governor Jim Pillen approved them 1 July 2026; one secondary source says they "took permanent effect July 6" (2026). Sources: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/nebraska-medical-marijuana-rule-updates-delayed-until-2027-as-10-manufacturer-applications-are-submitted/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://www.marijuanamoment.net/nebraska-officials-consider-changes-to-medical-marijuana-rules/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify the exact effective date (6 July vs 1 July) against the Secretary of State's published Title 238.
  • Next rulemaking: on 17 August 2026 the Commission advanced amendments including a proposed fee schedule; a public hearing was set for 15 October 2026; final amended regulations are not expected until 2027, and the Commission has said it will not license manufacturers or dispensaries until fees are authorised. Source: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/nebraska-medical-marijuana-rule-updates-delayed-until-2027-as-10-manufacturer-applications-are-submitted/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Manufacturer applications: 10 product-manufacturer applications had been submitted by the 14 September 2026 Commission meeting; one secondary source reports the window was extended a third time to 14 December 2026 while the Commission site still showed 14 September — Verify the current deadline directly with the Commission before relying on either date. Sources: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/nebraska-medical-marijuana-rule-updates-delayed-until-2027-as-10-manufacturer-applications-are-submitted/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://thecannabisbusinessadvisors.com/cannabis-licensing-update-week-of-september-21-2026/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

0.5 Litigation — signature challenge finally decided for the initiatives

  • Kuehn v. Evnen, S-24-901, 322 Neb. 161 (4 Sept 2026): the Nebraska Supreme Court unanimously (7-0, opinion by Chief Justice Jeffrey Funke) affirmed the Lancaster County District Court, holding opponents had not shown enough invalid petition signatures to disqualify either measure. Source (citation and docket from Justia's 2026 Nebraska Supreme Court index): https://law.Justia.com/cases/nebraska/supreme-court/2026/ — accessed 2026-10-30; reporting: https://www.marijuanamoment.net/nebraska-supreme-court-upholds-medical-marijuana-ballot-initiatives-rejecting-legal-challenge/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify the full opinion text on the Court's own site before quoting holdings verbatim.
  • A second Kuehn lawsuit challenging the constitutionality of the medical cannabis laws was also resolved in the initiatives' favour in September 2026: secondary reporting states the justices "unanimously affirmed the dismissal of another Kuehn lawsuit trying to challenge the constitutionality of the same medical cannabis laws." Source: https://couriernebraska.com/news/politics/nebraskas-fall-2026-ballot-lawsuits/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify — docket number, citation, and whether any Supremacy Clause / federal-law claim survives were not confirmed from a court source in this run.
  • Earlier district-court history: Lancaster County District Judge Susan Strong ruled 26 November 2024 that the measures fulfilled state requirements (signature sufficiency), and on 12 December 2024 rejected a request to block Governor Pillen's proclamation. Sources: https://www.cannabisbusinesstimes.com/top-stories/news/15710193/nebraska-medical-marijuana-legalization-measures-face-another-lawsuit — accessed 2026-10-30; https://gray-ksnb-prod.cdn.arcpublishing.com/2024/12/11/new-lawsuit-seeks-void-nebraska-medical-cannabis-ballot-measures/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

0.7 Attorney General hemp enforcement — the live market, not the medical programme

  • AG Mike Hilgers' office announced expansion of its delta-8 / synthetic-THC enforcement with cease-and-desist letters to 104 retail locations/franchised stores in Omaha (2025 announcement, opened directly), after test purchases from stores owned by 35 companies in which "[e]ach store investigated had at least one product marketed as hemp, which actually had a delta-9 concentration over the legal limit, making it marijuana." Source (official AG release, opened directly): https://ago.nebraska.gov/news/announcement-new-developments-delta-8-enforcement-efforts — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • By that announcement the office had filed 15 lawsuits under the Consumer Protection Act (unfairness and deception) and the Nebraska Pure Food Act, with 12 settled and 3 actively litigated. A later secondary update (29 Sept 2025) reports 16 lawsuits, 12 settlements, and letters to companies operating about 300 stores — the 15→16 difference is a timing difference; Verify the current count from the AG's office. Sources: AG release above; https://horganlawfirm.com/delta-8-thc-and-cbd-in-nebraska-a-guide-for-businesses/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Governor Pillen signed Executive Order 26-02 on 26 January 2026, "Protecting Nebraskans from Intoxicating Synthetic THC Products," directing agencies (Agriculture, Banking and Finance, Revenue) to review law/rules so consumable products do not contain intoxicating synthetic THC. Source: Governor's executive-order index, http://govdocs.nebraska.gov/docs/pilot/pubs/eoindex.html — accessed 2026-10-30; secondary summary: https://horganlawfirm.com/delta-8-thc-and-cbd-in-nebraska-a-guide-for-businesses/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • The Department of Agriculture then proposed 19 NAC 5 (draft, May 2026; hearing 18 June 2026) that would treat food, beverages, gummies and tinctures containing any amount of THC as adulterated, with an exception for the medical cannabis Acts. It is NOT final as of this run — Verify its current status. Source (secondary): https://horganlawfirm.com/delta-8-thc-and-cbd-in-nebraska-a-guide-for-businesses/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://gray-wowt-prod.gtv-cdn.com/2026/06/18/nebraska-dept-agriculture-proposes-ban-food-beverages-containing-any-amount-thc/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

0.8 2026 ballot — no cannabis measure; election not yet held as of 30 Oct 2026

  • The 3 November 2026 election has not yet been held as of 30 October 2026, so no 2026 result is reported here. The certified 2026 Nebraska ballot carries four measures — LRCA term limits, Initiative 440 (online sports betting authorisation), Initiative 441 (online sports betting regulation), and Initiative 442 (sex-based school sports teams) — and no cannabis measure. A 2026 citizen initiative to create a constitutional right to use cannabis did not make the ballot (signature deadline 3 July 2026). Sources: https://ballotpedia.org/Nebraska_2026_ballot_measures — accessed 2026-10-30; https://drugpolicywatch.info/states/nebraska.html — accessed 2026-10-30; https://sassa.unl.edu/2026-ballot-issues/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

PART 1 — LAWS

1.1 Legal status: what is legal, what the Commission regulates, what remains illegal

Legal for qualified patients and caregivers (state law only). Initiative 437, codified as the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act, Neb. Rev. Stat. §§71-24,103 to 71-24,105 (see §1.2), makes state and local penalties inapplicable to a qualified patient's use, possession and acquisition of up to five ounces of cannabis, and to a caregiver's possessing/acquiring/delivering that amount on the patient's behalf. Source (operative statute, opened directly): https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,105 — accessed 2026-10-30. Verbatim text is quoted in §1.2.

What the Commission regulates. Initiative 438, codified as the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act, Neb. Rev. Stat. §§71-24,106 to 71-24,111 (as amended by LB 1235), creates the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission to register and regulate private entities that possess, manufacture, distribute, deliver and dispense cannabis for medical purposes. Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,109 — accessed 2026-10-30 (creation and membership); definitions at https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,107 — accessed 2026-10-30.

What remains illegal for everyone else (state law). Adult-use / recreational cannabis is illegal in Nebraska. There is no adult-use statute, no adult-use licence type in law, and the 2026 adult-use constitutional initiative did not reach the ballot (see §0.8). Non-patient possession is penalised under §28-416 as set out in §1.3. Cultivation by anyone other than a Commission-licensed cultivator is unlawful; patient home cultivation is NOT authorised (see §1.4). Sale of marijuana outside the (not yet operational) licensed system is a felony under §28-416(1)–(2) (manufacture/distribution/delivery/dispensing of a Schedule I/II/III controlled substance — Class IIA felony for a non-exceptionally-hazardous Schedule I substance, subject to the subsections cited). Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=28-416 — accessed 2026-10-30.

Federal position — apply the fixed baseline carefully (flagged Verify where text does not settle it). - If/when a Nebraska Commission licensee is licensed and operating: under the fixed baseline, that licensee's state-licensed medical activity falls in federal Schedule III effective 28 April 2026, and 280E is generally lifted for that Schedule III medical activity. As of 30 Oct 2026 no licensee is yet operating a sale (see §0.1), so this Schedule III / 280E treatment has no operating Nebraska medical business to attach to yet; the four licensed cultivators are licensed but, on the evidence in this run, have not sold through a licensed manufacturer/dispensary chain. Verify whether any cultivator has lawfully transferred product to any licensed party, and whether IRS guidance treats pre-operational / cultivation-only activity the same way — the baseline text supplied does not settle the cultivation-only question. - Patients possessing cannabis under Initiative 437 with no state-licensed source: state law protects the possession itself (up to 5 oz, with a written recommendation), but it creates no lawful in-state source until dispensaries open. Federally, marijuana that is not state-licensed medical product or an FDA-approved product remains Schedule I under the fixed baseline. Verify — the federal rule's text as supplied does not settle whether a Nebraska patient's possession, absent any state-licensed supply, is treated as Schedule III medical activity or as Schedule I possession, and federal enforcement discretion / the appropriations rider (through 11 Dec 2026) is a separate question from scheduling. Do not present patient possession as federally lawful. - Any non-medical possession remains Schedule I federally under the fixed baseline, regardless of Nebraska's state infraction for ≤1 oz (see §1.3). Source for the federal baseline: task-supplied fixed context (not re-researched), applied 2026-10-30.

1.2 Every statute and regulation — citation, summary, verbatim operative text, last-amended date, official link

How to read the "last-amended" column: dates below are the source lines shown on the Legislature's statute pages as opened on 2026-10-30, or the bill-history dates for LBs. Where the statute page's full source line was not captured in the opened view, that is flagged Verify rather than guessed.

A. Initiative 437 — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act (Neb. Rev. Stat. §§71-24,103–71-24,105)

  • Citation / codification: Initiative Law 2024, No. 437, §§1–3, codified at Neb. Rev. Stat. §§71-24,103 (citation), 71-24,104 (definitions), 71-24,105 (protection). Amended in one technical respect by LB 1 (2025) (incorrect subdivision reference). Source for codification: §71-24,105 source line "Initiative Law 2024, No. 437, § 3," https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,105 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Plain summary: No list of qualifying conditions. Any person 18+ with a written recommendation, or a minor with a written recommendation plus parent/guardian permission, is a "qualified patient" and may use/possess/acquire up to 5 oz; caregivers (21+ designee, parent/guardian, or designated facility/agency) may do the same on the patient's behalf. No state patient registry or ID card is created by this Act — see §1.4.
  • Verbatim operative text — definitions (§71-24,104), opened directly: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,104 — accessed 2026-10-30.

    "(1)(a) Allowable amount of cannabis means up to five ounces of cannabis." "(b) Allowable amount of cannabis does not include the weight of any other ingredient combined with cannabis as part of topical or oral administrations, food, drink, or other preparations;" "(2)(a) Cannabis means all parts of the plant of the genus cannabis whether growing or not, the seeds thereof, the resin extracted from any part of the plant, and every compound, manufacture, salt, derivative, mixture, or preparation of the plant, its seeds, or its resin." "(b) Cannabis includes marijuana, hashish, and concentrated cannabis." "(c) Cannabis does not include hemp, as defined in section 2-503, nor does it include the mature stalks of the plant, fiber produced from such stalks, oil or cake made from the seeds of the plant, the sterilized seed of the plant which is incapable of germination, or cannabidiol contained in a drug product approved by the federal Food and Drug Administration;" "(3) Cannabis accessories means any equipment, products, or materials of any kind that are used, intended for use, or designed for use in storing, vaporizing, or containing cannabis, or for ingesting, inhaling, or otherwise introducing cannabis into the human body;" "(4) Caregiver means: (a) In the case of a qualified patient who is eighteen years of age or older and is not under the protection of a legal guardian, a person who: (i) Is at least twenty-one years of age; and (ii) Has been designated by a qualified patient in a signed affidavit; (b) In the case of a qualified patient who is younger than eighteen years of age or a qualified patient under the protection of a legal guardian: (i) The legal guardian or parent with authority to make health care decisions of the qualified patient; or (ii) A person designated in a sworn affidavit by the legal guardian or parent with authority to make health care decisions; or (c) A health care facility as defined in section 71-413 or a home health agency as defined in section 71-417, if the facility or agency has been designated by a qualified patient or the legal guardian or parent with authority to make health care decisions of a qualified patient in a sworn affidavit and if the facility or agency has agreed in writing to serve as a caregiver for the qualified patient;" "(5) Health care practitioner means a physician, an osteopathic physician, a physician assistant, or a nurse practitioner licensed under the Uniform Credentialing Act or who is licensed in any state and practicing in compliance with the Uniform Credentialing Act;" "(6) Qualified patient means: (a) An individual eighteen years of age or older with a written recommendation from a health care practitioner; or (b) An individual younger than eighteen years of age with a written recommendation from a health care practitioner and with the written permission of a legal guardian or parent with authority to make health care decisions for the individual; and" "(7) Written recommendation means a valid signed and dated declaration from a health care practitioner stating that, in the health care practitioner's professional judgment, the potential benefits of cannabis outweigh the potential harms for the alleviation of a patient's medical condition, its symptoms, or side effects of the condition's treatment. A written recommendation is valid for two years after the date of issuance or for a period of time specified by the health care practitioner on the written recommendation."

  • Verbatim operative text — protection (§71-24,105), opened directly: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,105 — accessed 2026-10-30.

    "(1) Subject to the requirements of the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act, it shall not be an offense under state or local law for: (a) A qualified patient to use, possess, and acquire an allowable amount of cannabis and cannabis accessories for the alleviation of a medical condition, its symptoms, or side effects of the condition's treatment; or (b) A caregiver to assist a qualified patient with the activities set forth in subdivision (1)(a) of this section by possessing and acquiring an allowable amount of cannabis and cannabis accessories on behalf of the qualified patient and delivering an allowable amount of cannabis and cannabis accessories to the qualified patient." "(2) Conduct protected by this section shall not be subject to the Uniform Controlled Substances Act."

  • Last amended: LB 1 (2025) technical correction — Verify LB 1's final enactment/chapter date from its bill history; the engrossed copy is at https://leg.ne.gov/FloorDocs/109/PDF/Engrossed/LB1.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. Ballot text (for reference, from the Secretary of State pamphlet): "Shall a statute be enacted that makes penalties inapplicable under state and local law for the use, possession, and acquisition of an allowable amount (up to five ounces) of cannabis for medical purposes by a qualified patient with a written recommendation from a health care practitioner, and for a caregiver to assist a qualified patient with these activities?" Source: https://sos.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/elections/2024/2024%20Ballot%20Measures%20Pamphlet.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.

B. Initiative 438 — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act (Neb. Rev. Stat. §§71-24,106–71-24,111)

  • Citation / codification: Initiative Law 2024, No. 438, codified at §§71-24,106 (citation) to 71-24,111; §71-24,109 (Commission) amended by LB 1235 (2026), operative 8 April 2026. Sources: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,109 — accessed 2026-10-30; slip law http://nebraskalegislature.gov/FloorDocs/109/PDF/Slip/LB1235.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Plain summary: Creates the Commission; defines "registered cannabis establishment" and "registration"; requires the Commission to establish criteria to accept/deny registrations and to begin issuing registrations. The initiative's own deadlines, as secondarily reported, were: criteria/rules by 1 July 2025 and registrations beginning by 1 October 2025. The 1 October 2025 deadline was missed for the first licences by about a week (first offers 7 October 2025) and has not been met at all for manufacturers/dispensaries. Sources for the deadlines: https://www.cannabisbusinesstimes.com/top-stories/news/15707585/nebraska-voters-pass-medical-cannabis-legalization-measures-litigation-remains-ongoing — accessed 2026-10-30; https://hempgazette.com/news/nebraska-cannabis-regulations-hg2503/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify the deadline language in the full text of §§71-24,108/71-24,110/71-24,111, which were not directly opened in this run.
  • Verbatim operative text — definitions (§71-24,107), opened directly: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,107 — accessed 2026-10-30.

    "(1)(a) Cannabis means all parts of the plant of the genus cannabis whether growing or not, the seeds thereof, the resin extracted from any part of the plant, and every compound, manufacture, salt, derivative, mixture, or preparation of the plant, its seeds, or its resin. (b) Cannabis includes marijuana, hashish, and concentrated cannabis. (c) Cannabis does not include hemp, as defined in section 2-503, nor does it include the mature stalks of the plant, fiber produced from such stalks, oil or cake made from the seeds of the plant, the sterilized seed of the plant which is incapable of germination, or cannabidiol contained in a drug product approved by the federal Food and Drug Administration;" "(4) Cannabis products means products that are comprised of cannabis, cannabis concentrate, or cannabis extract, and other ingredients, and that are intended for use or consumption, such as, but not limited to, edible products, ointments, and tinctures;" "(6) Registered cannabis establishment means a private entity registered pursuant to the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act that possesses, manufactures, distributes, delivers, or dispenses cannabis for medical purposes; and" "(7) Registration means a registration granted by the commission to a private entity that processes, manufactures, distributes, delivers, or dispenses cannabis for medical purposes." Note the tension to flag, not resolve: §71-24,107(4) expressly contemplates "edible products" as cannabis products, while Commission regulations prohibit dispensary sale of edibles and raw flower (see §1.3). The Commission's own position, as secondarily reported, is that the regulations govern what establishments may sell, not what patients may possess under Initiative 437. Source: https://www.omahadailyrecord.com/content/medical-cannabis-liquor-control-commissions-seek-combine-resources-frustrations-flare — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify whether any court has ruled on this tension — none was located in this run.

  • Verbatim operative text — Commission (§71-24,109), opened directly: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,109 — accessed 2026-10-30.

    "(1) For purposes of providing the necessary registration and regulation of persons that possess, manufacture, distribute, deliver, and dispense cannabis for medical purposes pursuant to the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act, the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission is created." "(2) The commission shall consist of no fewer than three and no more than five members." "(3) The three members of the Nebraska Liquor Control Commission shall be ex officio members of the commission, serving terms and receiving appointment in the same manner as provided in section 53-105." "(4) The Governor may appoint two additional members, subject to confirmation by a majority of the members elected to the Legislature, to serve with the members of the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission. The members appointed pursuant to this subsection shall serve six-year terms." "(6) Each member of the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission shall receive an annual salary of twelve thousand five hundred dollars. Such salary shall be paid in equal installments." [Salary added by LB 1235; source line: "Initiative Law 2024, No. 438, § 4; Laws 2026, LB1235, § 6. Operative Date: April 8, 2026."]

  • Last amended: LB 1235, approved by the Governor 7 April 2026, operative 8 April 2026 (for §71-24,109). Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,109 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Ballot text (438, from the Secretary of State pamphlet): "Shall a statute be enacted that makes penalties inapplicable under state law for the possession, manufacture, distribution, delivery, and dispensing of cannabis for medical purposes by registered private entities, and that establishes a Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission to regulate such activities?" Source: https://sos.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/elections/2024/2024%20Ballot%20Measures%20Pamphlet.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.

C. Uniform Controlled Substances Act (Neb. Rev. Stat. §§28-401.01 et seq.)

  • §28-401 — Terms, defined (official page located via search; full official text not extracted in this run — key excerpts from the official page's search rendering, Verify against the opened page): "Controlled substance does not include distilled spirits, wine, malt beverages, tobacco, hemp, or any nonnarcotic substance…" and "(c) Marijuana does not include hemp." Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=28-401 (located; cited via search rendering) — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify by opening the page directly before republication.
  • §28-405 — Schedules. Marijuana / tetrahydrocannabinols are listed in Schedule I, subdivision (c)(28) (the subdivision cross-referenced throughout §28-416). The current §28-416 text opened directly repeatedly cross-references "subdivision (c)(28) of Schedule I of section 28-405." Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=28-416 — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify the full Schedule I tetrahydrocannabinol listing in §28-405 itself — it was not directly opened. Note: the fixed federal baseline (Schedule III for state-licensed medical, 28 Apr 2026) is a federal scheduling action; it does not by itself amend Nebraska's state Schedule I listing. Whether Nebraska has conformed its state schedule is Verify — no Nebraska schedule-conforming amendment was located in this run.
  • §28-416 — Prohibited acts; violations; penalties (opened directly, full operative text in §1.3 below).   • §28-441 — Drug paraphernalia (opened via official page rendering): "(1) It shall be unlawful for any person to use, or to possess with intent to use, drug paraphernalia to manufacture, inject, ingest, inhale, or otherwise introduce into the human body a controlled substance in violation of sections 28-101, 28-431, and 28-439 to 28-444. (2) Any person who violates this section shall be guilty of an infraction. (3) A person shall not be in violation of this section if section 28-472 or 28-1701 applies." Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=28-441 — accessed 2026-10-30. Last amendments shown: Laws 2022, LB 519, § 5 (effective 21 July 2022, per secondary rendering — Verify).
  • §28-1701 and §28-472: repeatedly cross-referenced in §§28-416 and 28-441 as carve-outs ("A person shall not be in violation of this subsection if section 28-472 or 28-1701 applies"). Their full texts were not opened in this run — Unavailable/Verify: do not describe their content beyond the carve-out function shown in the opened statutes. This matters because one or both may bear on medical-cannabis or hemp possession defences; Run 2 / follow-up must open https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=28-1701 and …?statute=28-472 directly.
  • §77-4303 (marijuana and controlled substances tax): referenced in LB 1235's title as amended (sales-tax-related sections) but its cannabis-tax operation is Part 2 territory — noted here only as a cross-reference; Verify in Part 2.

D. Nebraska Hemp Farming Act (Neb. Rev. Stat. §§2-501–2-518)

  • Origin: LB 657 (2019). Later amendments located: LB 1152 (2020); LB 263 (2023, USDA Final Rule conformity); LB 262 (2024). Source for the amendment chain on §2-503: "Laws 2019, LB657, § 3; Laws 2020, LB1152, § 1; … Laws 2024, LB262, § 2," https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=2-503 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Verbatim operative definition (§2-503, current numbering — note: older sources cite §2-503(13); current sources cite §2-503(3); Verify the current subdivision number on the opened page):

    "Hemp means the plant Cannabis sativa L. and any part of such plant, including the viable seeds of such plant and all derivatives, extracts, cannabinoids, isomers, acids, salts, and salts of isomers, whether growing or not, with a delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol concentration of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis. Hemp shall be considered an agricultural commodity. Notwithstanding any other provision of law, hemp shall not be considered a controlled substance under the Uniform Controlled Substances Act;" Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=2-503 — accessed 2026-10-30.

  • THC measurement (state law, pre-federal-change): delta-9 THC only, ≤0.3% on a dry-weight basis. The Act incorporates federal concepts: "Acceptable hemp THC level has the same meaning as in 7 C.F.R. 990.1" (as of the dates stated in the statute). Whether Nebraska's hemp programme today is a USDA-approved state plan or USDA-direct licensing is Part 7 territory — but for Part 1, note the secondary report that "A 2024 amendment (LB 262) shifted hemp producer licensing to the USDA" (https://horganlawfirm.com/delta-8-thc-and-cbd-in-nebraska-a-guide-for-businesses/ — accessed 2026-10-30). Verify against the Nebraska Department of Agriculture / USDA AMS list before stating the plan status as fact.
  • Later hemp law: LB 316 would have redefined hemp and restricted non-CBD hemp products — it is not law (indefinitely postponed 17 Apr 2026, see §1.9). No other enacted post-2019 hemp restriction statute was located in this run; the operative restrictions in 2026 are executive (EO 26-02) and proposed-rule (19 NAC 5, not final) actions plus AG enforcement under pre-existing consumer-protection / Pure Food law — see §1.5. Verify by a full §§2-501–2-518 read; this run opened §2-503 only.

  • Emergency regulations: first adopted summer 2025 (Commission approval; Governor Pillen approval ahead of 1 July 2025 deadline; 90-day duration, renewable once for 90 days); extended 16 April 2026 (Secretary of State emergency file).
