MahāMotā Cultivation Company in Raymond (Lancaster County) had its inspection ratified unanimously by the Commission on 22 June 2026, clearing it to plant — the first state-licensed cultivator cleared to put plants in the ground. No Raymond-specific ordinance was located; this is a state inspection outcome, not a local rule. — North Platte Post, June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30.

Bans / moratoriums / caps found at local level:

  • No local ban on medical cannabis establishments and no local moratorium was confirmed for any jurisdiction checked. Omaha's chapter regulates rather than bans; Bridgeport's ordinance regulates (with a conditional-use permit) rather than bans; Buffalo County's proposal would require a special-use permit rather than ban; Gretna granted a permit. The only local "stop" found is the Washington County administrator's agricultural-exemption reversal/stop-work order against one cultivator (above), which is an individual zoning determination under appeal, not a jurisdiction-wide ban or moratorium.
  • No local cap (e.g. "no more than X dispensaries in this city") was located for any jurisdiction checked. The only caps found are state caps (1 dispensary per judicial district; 4 cultivators statewide — see § 3.2). Verify for Omaha/Bridgeport code text beyond the summaries below, in case a cap is embedded in a district table not excerpted.

3.4 Local zoning, hours, buffers and fees — adopted ordinances in detail

Omaha — Ordinance 44411 (new Omaha Municipal Code chapter), passed 30 September 2025.

  • The Omaha City Council agenda/journal record identifies Ord. 44411 as "an ordinance to create a new Chapter in the Omaha Municipal Code governing local activities permitted under Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act (hereinafter, the 'NMCRA') and the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act (hereinafter, the 'NMCPPA') … mandating that any cannabis establishment registered with the State of Nebraska … shall comply with all relevant state laws, rules and regulations, or emergency rules and regulations, and local ordinances," with first reading 16 Sep 2025, second reading/public hearing 23 Sep 2025, third reading 30 Sep 2025. — City of Omaha, City Council Agenda, 30 Sep 2025 (PDF) — accessed 2026-10-30; and City of Omaha, Journal Record, 30 Sep 2025 (PDF) — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Nebraska Public Media's report of the unanimous vote states: "dispensaries and other cannabis businesses like manufacturers, producers and transporters, will not be permitted to operate in residential areas. Cannabis consumption will not be allowed onsite at any cannabis businesses, and they must open no earlier than 8 a.m. and close no later than 11 p.m." and "The regulations do not make any changes to the city's zoning code." — Nebraska Public Media, "City of Omaha approves zoning regulations for medical cannabis businesses" — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • District rules as explained by the City Law Department at the public hearing and reported by Nebraska Public Media: "Dispensaries will be allowed to operate in general retail sales districts. Indoor cultivators can operate in commercial, office and industrial districts, while outdoor cultivators can operate in agriculture districts if they are more than 20 acres in size. Cannabis manufacturers can operate in areas zoned for light industry, while transporters can operate in areas zoned for warehousing and distribution." — Nebraska Public Media, same article — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Verify: The full text of Ord. 44411 / the new chapter number was not opened in full for this Part (the Daily Record legal-notice page excerpt confirms passage on 30 Sep 2025 but does not reproduce the chapter text). Local permit fees, application forms, and any local buffer distance beyond the state's 1,000-foot covered-location rule are therefore Unavailable / Verify for Omaha. The state 1,000-foot rule (§ 3.2) applies in Omaha regardless.
  • Local effect on a licensee: Midwest Cultivator Group "faced changing zoning requirements in Omaha" and relocated to Gretna (see Gretna row above), showing Omaha's rules had a practical siting effect even before any dispensary opened. — MJBizDaily, 23 June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30.

Bridgeport (Morrill County) — Ordinance 1032, passed and approved 13 November 2025, amending City Code Chapter 153 (Zoning Code).

  • Ord. 1032 "adds a section to Chapter 153: Zoning Code, to set forth Cannabis Establishment Regulations within the City," effective on passage, approval and publication in pamphlet form. — City of Bridgeport, Ordinance 1032 (PDF) — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • The codified sections are §§ 153.400–153.410, including: statement of purpose; cultivators (§ 153.402); product manufacturers (§ 153.403); dispensaries (§ 153.404); transporters (§ 153.405); "Conditional use permit required for medical cannabis establishments" (§ 153.406); distance requirements (§ 153.407); measurements (§ 153.408); other locational requirements (§ 153.409); unlicensed establishments (§ 153.410). — American Legal Publishing, Bridgeport NE Code, Chapter 153 index — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Definitions track state law: "Medical Cannabis Establishment means a medical cannabis cultivator, medical cannabis product manufacturer, medical cannabis dispensary, or medical cannabis transporter as those terms are defined in Neb. Rev. Stat. §§ 71-24,104 and 71-24,107 and Title 238 NAC 1," and "Covered Location means any school, childcare center or child day care, church, or hospital." — City of Bridgeport, Ordinance 1032 (PDF) — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Measurement: "One thousand (1,000) feet distances required herein will be measured in a straight line from the nearest property line of the parcel on which the covered location or residence is located to the nearest property line of the parcel on which the licensed premises of the medical cannabis establishment is located." — City of Bridgeport, Ordinance 1032 (PDF) — accessed 2026-10-30. Note this measures property-line to property-line, whereas the state rule measures property-line to perimeter wall (§ 3.2) — the stricter of the two will bind in Bridgeport in practice; this difference is flagged, not resolved, here.
  • Other locational rules in the ordinance excerpt: "No medical cannabis establishment shall relocate from the place specified in its state license. Vertical licensing is prohibited, and no entity may possess more than one license type," and unlicensed establishments are prohibited. — City of Bridgeport, Ordinance 1032 (PDF) — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Verify / Unavailable: The full district-by-district table (which zoning districts allow which establishment type) and the conditional-use-permit fee for Bridgeport were not in the excerpt opened; they are Verify from the full § 153.402–153.407 text.

Gretna — conditional-use permit (no ordinance text located).

  • Gretna's planning committee (28 Apr 2026) and City Council (19 May 2026) each voted unanimously to approve a conditional-use permit for Midwest Cultivator Group's industry-park site, as reported to the Commission. — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission, Meeting Minutes, 22 June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30. The permit fee, the ordinance under which it was granted, and whether Gretna has a general medical-cannabis chapter are Verify.

Buffalo County — special-use permit proposal (16 July 2026, adoption Verify).

  • See table row above. No fee stated in the minutes excerpt — Unavailable / Verify.

Local permit fees — summary:

  • Omaha: Unavailable (no fee in the sources opened).
  • Bridgeport: Unavailable (conditional-use permit required, fee not in excerpt).
  • Gretna: Unavailable (permit granted, fee not stated).
  • Buffalo County: Unavailable (special-use permit proposed, fee not stated).
  • All other required jurisdictions: Unavailable — no local medical-cannabis permit fee was located, because no local medical-cannabis permit scheme was located for them.
  • The only fee firmly tied to siting in this Part is the state $1,500 relocation fee (§ 3.2), which is not a local fee.

3.5 Business concentration — ONLY what official sources say

  • No official source located states where licensed medical cannabis businesses are concentrated, clustered or ranked. This Part therefore gives no ranking and no cluster estimate, as instructed.
  • What official/Commission sources do say about locations (all cultivators; no dispensary location can be stated because no dispensary licence had been issued — see Part 6):
  • MahāMotā Cultivation Company — Raymond, Lancaster County — first cultivator cleared to plant (Commission, 22 June 2026). — North Platte Post, June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Midwest Cultivator Group — relocated from Omaha (Douglas County) to Gretna (Sarpy County), industry park, conditional-use permit approved — Commission-approved relocation (22 June 2026). — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission, Meeting Minutes, 22 June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • KRL Med LLC — Herman, Washington County (Parcel ID 890087032, County Road P6, Herman, NE 68029 in its own county filing; the site is the subject of the Washington County zoning dispute above) — location from the company's own filing as reported in secondary coverage; Verify the current site status, as the stop-work order/appeal may have changed it.
  • Meadowlark Medicinals — location Unavailable in sources opened (inspection delayed, no site named in the June 2026 meeting reports).
  • Dispensary geography is fixed by the state rule, not by local concentration: one dispensary per judicial district (12 districts), so by design no district — including Douglas (Omaha) or Lancaster (Lincoln) — can host more than one dispensary under the current rule, subject to the possible additional licence on a sufficiency finding. — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission, Title 238 NAC 1, § 04.12 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Hemp geography: No official county-by-county list of hemp licensees/locations for 2025–2026 was located (USDA now licenses; see Part 6). Do not infer a hemp cluster from the cultivator sites above — those are medical cannabis, not hemp.

Part 4 — Licence Types

PART 4 — LICENCE TYPES

4.0 Bottom line up front

  • Licence types in the current (draft) regulations: three licences + one certification. No others. Cultivator, product manufacturer, and dispensary licences, plus a transportation certification that only a product-manufacturer licensee may hold. There is no standalone transporter licence and no testing-laboratory licence in the regulations read for this research.
  • Caps: 4 cultivators, 4 product manufacturers, 12 dispensaries (one per judicial district). Each cap may grow by one per year (per district, for dispensaries) if the Commission finds supply insufficient. Vertical integration is prohibited: one licence type per applicant.
  • Issued: 4 cultivator licences (cap full). 0 manufacturer licences. 0 dispensary licences. 0 transportation certifications. The "2 vs 4 cultivators" conflict is resolved as timing, not contradiction (§4.5).
  • Operating: 1 cultivator cleared to grow (MahāMotā Cultivation Company, Raymond). 0 businesses selling to patients. No licensed dispensary, manufacturer, or testing laboratory is operating, and no patient sale has lawfully occurred as of 2026-10-30. First patient sales are tentatively projected for spring 2027 in secondary reporting — a projection, not a Commission date.
  • Applications: cultivator window closed (September 2025). Manufacturer window opened June 2026 with 10 applications reported by 14 September 2026, but no licences issued and the window's closing date is CONFLICTED (14 September vs 14 December 2026) — Verify (§4.5). Dispensary applications have not opened; no date has been announced.
  • Social equity: NONE enacted. No social-equity licence, priority window, fee discount, or ownership preference exists in the initiatives, LB 1235 as enacted, or the draft regulations. The only equity-adjacent proposals (LB 705's reservations) were indefinitely postponed. A secondary summary claiming a January/April 2026 social-equity priority window is not supported by any official source located and is treated as erroneous (§4.7).
  • Adult-use licences: NONE exist and NONE are pending as a bill. The only 2026 adult-use vehicle was a citizen constitutional initiative that did not qualify for the 3 November 2026 ballot; LR 456 is an interim study only (§4.9).

4.1 Operating status — the answer to "is any licensee licensed AND operating?"

No licensee is licensed and operating a business that sells medical cannabis to patients. One licensee is licensed and growing. - Licensed AND growing: MahāMotā Cultivation Company (also rendered MahaMota / MahāMotā Cultivation Co.), Raymond, Nebraska. On 23 June 2026 the Commission "voted unanimously to ratify the successful inspection of MahaMota Cultivation Co., the first licensed medical marijuana cultivator approved to start growing" — Source: https://mjbizdaily.com/news/nebraska-medical-marijuana-cultivator-finally-approved-to-start-growing/616598/ — accessed 2026-10-30; corroborated: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/06/nebraska-commission-clears-first-legal-marijuana-cultivation-opens-door-for-product-manufacturer-applications/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("ratify the successful inspection of MahāMotā Cultivation Company in Raymond, allowing the company to begin cultivation… the first state-licensed cultivator cleared to put marijuana plants in the ground"). As of the Commission's 19 October 2026 meeting, MahāMotā was "the only one of four licensed growers with plants currently in the ground" with "its first harvest expected within seven weeks" of that meeting — Source: https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884587/nebraska-cannabis-cultivators-may-wait-two-years-for-testing-guidelines/ — accessed 2026-10-30. - Licensed but NOT growing / NOT selling: the other three cultivators (see §4.5) — blocked by a Washington County zoning dispute (KRL Med LLC), a delayed inspection (Meadowlark Medicinals LLC), and a relocation from Omaha to Gretna (Midwest Cultivators Group) respectively, per the June 2026 reporting above. - No dispensary is licensed, so no dispensary is operating. No product manufacturer is licensed, so no manufacturer is operating. No testing laboratory is licensed under the medical programme (there is no such licence type — §4.3). It follows that no lawful patient sale has occurred, and no licensee is currently in a position to claim the federal Schedule III / §280E benefit on sales income (§2.6). Secondary summaries agree: "no dispensary has opened, no manufacturer licensing process has been finalized" (CannBus Q3 2026 — Source: https://www.cannbus.org/lsum/ne_lsum.html — accessed 2026-10-30); "Nebraska has yet to license testing laboratories, manufacturers or dispensaries, leaving growers without a clear path to market" (MJBizDaily, 21 October 2026 — Source: https://mjbizdaily.com/news/nebraska-cannabis-growers-face-up-to-2-year-wait-to-sell-products/618795/ — accessed 2026-10-30). - Schedule III / §280E application to the one growing licensee: MahāMotā, as a state-licensed medical cultivator, falls within the fixed federal baseline's Schedule III category (state-licensed medical marijuana) once it holds product for medical purposes; but with no manufacturer, testing, or dispensary downstream and no sales, there is currently no medical-cannabis sales income to which §280E relief would apply, and its pre-revenue cultivation costs' federal treatment is entity- and fact-specific — Verify with a tax adviser. This report does not advise that MahāMotā (or any cultivator) is currently realising a §280E benefit.

4.2 Legal basis — initiatives, statute, and regulations (what each layer does)

  • Initiative 437 — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Patient Protection Act, codified at Neb. Rev. Stat. §§ 71-24,103–71-24,105. Protects qualified patients/caregivers possessing up to 5 ounces with a health-care practitioner's written recommendation. It does not itself create business licences.
  • Initiative 438 — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Regulation Act, codified at Neb. Rev. Stat. §§ 71-24,106–71-24,111. Creates the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission and authorises it to register/regulate "registered cannabis establishments". All licence types below derive from this Act as implemented by Commission regulation — not from any later legislative framework bill, because every 2025 framework bill (LB 651, LB 677, LB 705) was indefinitely postponed (§2.7).
  • LB 1235 (enacted 2026) — the only enacted legislative amendment to the programme framework in this period. It does not create new licence types; it funds and staffs the Commission, caps fees at $50,000 each, creates the Cash Fund, and mandates fingerprint background checks (§2.2). The Commission's own meeting record describes LB 1235's earlier form as having contemplated patient/caregiver/practitioner regulation that was removed before passage; the enacted version must not be described as creating a patient registry — no patient registry exists under enacted law.
  • Commission regulations — Title 238, Nebraska Administrative Code, Chapter 1 (Registered Cannabis Establishments). History: emergency regulations approved 26 June 2025 (Governor sign-off 29 June 2025, effective 90 days, successively extended); formal/permanent regulations approved by the Commission 13 April 2026 and signed off by the Attorney General and Governor in summer 2026 (reported as "end of June" in Nebraska Public Media and "July 1, 2026" in secondary summaries — exact sign-off date Verify, but the permanent package's existence by summer 2026 is corroborated by the Commission's first post-approval meeting in July 2026, at which it updated the regulations with fees and tracking rules — Source: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/07/nebraska-medical-cannabis-commission-approves-new-licensing-fees-tracking-and-transportation-rules/ — accessed 2026-10-30). The fee/tracking update voted 17 August 2026 is a further amendment to that package and is itself pending final approval (§2.2, Layer 3). The draft text read for this research is the 21 August 2026 draft (file name "DRAFT.8.21.final.pdf"), which is the fullest available text of the pending package; where it differs from the summer-2026 permanent package (notably by adding the $5,000 application fee and the $13,000 tracking fee), the draft is the pending text and the permanent text is the currently effective text — the distinction matters for whether a fee is currently payable, and is flagged wherever a fee is cited.

4.3 Licence-type table — every type, what it allows, caps, fees, requirements

Common rules (all licence types, draft 238 NAC 1): - No vertical licensing. "Vertical licensing is not permitted. A [cultivator / dispensary / product manufacturer] applicant may not possess more than one license type authorized by this chapter." — §§ 04.01, 05.01, 06.01 — Source: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/Revised%20-%20238%20NAC%201%20DRAFT.8.21.final.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. A business may therefore not hold, for example, both a cultivator and a dispensary licence. (The 2025 framework bills' "vertical facility" concept was never enacted.) - Licences are non-transferable. §§ 04.11, 05.11, 06.11 ("Licenses are non-transferrable"; for manufacturers, "Licenses and transportation certifications are nontransferrable") — same source. - Relocation only on showing "an exceptional change of circumstances" and Commission approval, with a $1,500 relocation fee (PROPOSED, draft) — §§ 04.09–04.10, 05.09–05.10, 06.09–06.10 — same source. Midwest Cultivators Group's Omaha-to-Gretna relocation was approved under this mechanism in June 2026 (see §4.5). - 1,000-foot buffer: "No [dispensary / cultivator / manufacturer] license will be issued for any premises located within one thousand feet of any covered location" (covered location = school, licensed child-care business, church, hospital, or mental-health/substance-use treatment centre as defined in § 02.10), measured "in a straight line from the nearest property line of the covered location to the nearest perimeter wall of the licensed premises", except for an establishment already in operation before the covered location was established — §§ 04.08, 05.08, 06.08 — same source. - Residency / citizenship (all types): individual applicants must be U.S. citizens resident in Nebraska for at least 4 years immediately preceding application; entity applicants must be majority-owned by U.S. citizens resident in Nebraska for at least 4 years, and their officers and directors must have been Nebraska residents for at least 4 years. "Majority owned means more than fifty percent (50%) of the financial interests (other than a security interest, lien, or encumbrance) or more than fifty percent (50%) of the voting interests of an entity, including any parent and subsidiary entities." Proof is by three of: Nebraska vehicle registration; owned/rented Nebraska property; Nebraska mailing address; Nebraska address on state/federal tax returns; Nebraska voter registration; Nebraska driver's licence — §§ 04.03, 05.03, 06.03 — same source. Note a wording variance: the Commission's emergency-regulations "How to apply" page states the threshold as "more than fifty-one percent (51%)" — Source: https://lcc.nebraska.gov/medical-cannabis/medical-cannabis-commission-how-apply — accessed 2026-10-30. The 50% figure is from the pending draft; the 51% figure is from the emergency text. The difference (50% vs 51%) is flagged Verify for any applicant relying on a bare-majority structure; in practice both require a Nebraska-resident majority. - Prohibited persons (all types): no licence to or held by (A) a person convicted of any felony or any controlled-substance-related offence within the preceding 10 years (and no entity whose officers/directors/owners have such a conviction); (B/C) an entity whose officers/directors/owners have had a licence/permit suspended or revoked under these regulations; (D) a person under the age of majority; (E) any state/county/municipality or other government body; (F) "A health care practitioner who has issued one or more written recommendations or written orders in the preceding five (5) years"; or (G) a person who has had any citation, fine, sanction, injunction or court judgment levied against them (or a business they owned) involving cannabis or cannabinoid operations or sales — §§ 04.07, 05.07, 06.07 — same source. The 10-year felony/controlled-substance bar is the operative disqualifier most relevant to social-equity analysis (§4.7): it is a bar, not a preference. - Fingerprinting (all types): "An applicant for initial issuance of any license under this chapter shall also submit required fingerprints, to be furnished for every person included in the application, to the Nebraska State Patrol for a National criminal history record information check and a fee for such record check payable to the patrol" — §§ 04.02, 05.02, 06.02 — same source; now also required by LB 1235 § 9 (§2.2). - Business plan (all types): proof of financial capability to build, launch and sustain the establishment (capital, detailed budget, funding sources); facility design and operational readiness; residency demonstration; regulated-industry and cannabis experience; leadership qualifications; one-year financial projections — §§ 04.05, 05.05, 06.05 — same source. Manufacturers additionally must describe product types, extraction methods, equipment, sanitation/manufacturing safety, Good Manufacturing Practices and testing protocols, certifications pursued, and any contracted/subcontracted manufacturing/testing parties (§ 06.05). - Scoring / lottery (all types): applications are accepted only during a window published on the Commission's website (§ 03.01); if more applications are received than licences available, "the Commission will select applicants for available licenses by lottery" (§ 03.04), with anonymous identifiers, random drawing (separate drawings per judicial district where licences are issued by district), then merit review of drawn applications in drawn order; "Applications with an average score of more than 70 points out of 100 points will be eligible for licensure" (§ 03.04(E)), scored "with primary emphasis placed on comprehensive business plans, financial stability, facility design, and operational readiness". If fewer applications than licences are received, all compliant applications are reviewed without a lottery, on the same >70 standard (§ 03.05). Approved applicants have 5 business days to accept in writing (§ 03.07). One application per applicant per licence type per window, and one licence type per applicant per window; applications sharing officers/owners/minority owners are treated as the same applicant (§ 03.03(B)). Renewal: apply 30–90 days before expiry (§ 07.01). - Licence duration: 1 year if a fee is required; 6 months if no fee is required (§ 03.09) — same source. This is why the first cultivator licences, issued fee-free, expired after 6 months and required renewal in spring 2026.

Licence / certification What it allows (verbatim operative definition) Cap Licence fee (draft, PROPOSED) + renewal Issued / operating as of 2026-10-30
Cultivator "a registered establishment licensed to cultivate and process cannabis plants for the purposes of selling to Nebraska-licensed product manufacturers, and to other Nebraska-licensed cultivators, but cannot transfer cannabis plants or cannabis products to qualified patients, caregivers, or any other unlicensed entity not permitted under this chapter" (§ 02.11). Plant limit: "A cultivator may utilize any combination of indoor, outdoor, or greenhouse facilities, but may possess no more than one thousand two hundred fifty (1,250) flowering plants at one time" (§ 014.06). 4 statewide. "No more than four (4) cultivator licenses shall be issued in the state. If during any calendar year the Commission finds the number of licensed cultivators is not sufficient to meet the demand for medical cannabis or medical cannabis products, the Commission may grant an additional one (1) cultivator license during the following year." (§ 05.12) $20,000 issuance; $23,500 renewal (§§ 05.13, 07.02(A)) 4 issued (cap full); 1 growing (MahāMotā). See §4.5.
Product manufacturer (called "product manufacturer" in the regulations; "manufacturer" / "processor" in secondary coverage — same licence) "a registered cannabis establishment licensed to process cannabis, conduct extractions, and manufacture cannabis products for sale or transfer to dispensaries, but cannot sell or transfer cannabis plants or cannabis products to qualified patients, caregivers, or any other unlicensed entity not permitted under this chapter" (§ 02.28). 4 statewide. "No more than four (4) product manufacturer licenses shall be issued in the state" with the same +1-per-year-if-insufficient-demand mechanism (§ 06.12). $20,000 issuance; $23,500 renewal without transportation certification, $28,500 with it (§§ 06.13, 07.02(B)–(C)) 0 issued. 10 applications reported by 14 Sep 2026; window close date conflicted — Verify (§4.5).
Dispensary "a registered cannabis establishment licensed to possess and sell medical cannabis to qualified patients and caregivers" (§ 02.14). 12 statewide = 1 per district court judicial district (Neb. Rev. Stat. § 24-301.02). "No more than one (1) dispensary license shall be issued in any one district court judicial district… If during any calendar year the Commission finds the number of licensed dispensaries in any judicial district(s) is not sufficient to meet the demand for medical cannabis or medical cannabis products, the Commission may grant an additional one (1) dispensary license in the identified judicial district(s) during the following year." (§ 04.12) $15,000 issuance; $18,000 renewal (§§ 04.13, 07.02(D)) 0 issued. Applications not yet opened.
Transportation certification — NOT a standalone licence "Transporter means a registered cannabis establishment with a valid product manufacturer license and a valid transportation certification" (§ 02.35). "A product manufacturer license applicant may also seek a transportation certification. The transportation certification will permit product manufacturer licensees to transport medical cannabis, medical cannabis products, medical cannabis accessories, and medical cannabis waste between registered cannabis establishments, provide logistical services to other registered cannabis establishments, and store medical cannabis." (§ 06.14). Certification applications must be submitted with the manufacturer application and are treated as one application (§ 06.16). No cap stated in the draft regulations. The "12 transporters" figure appearing in secondary summaries (e.g. CannBus Q3 2026: "12 transporters" alongside 4/4/12) was NOT found in the draft text: a full-text search of the draft for "twelve" returns only unrelated provisions (12-month practitioner rule; 12-inch plant-tag rule). The 12-transporter figure is therefore Verify / treated as outdated — it may derive from an earlier emergency-regulation draft or from secondary conflation with the 12-dispensary cap. Do not publish "cap: 12 transporters" as current regulation without locating it in an official regulation text. $10,000 certification fee (§ 06.15(D)); renewal is folded into the manufacturer's $28,500 renewal rate (§ 07.02(C)) 0 issued (no manufacturer licensee exists to hold one).
Testing laboratory Testing is required — e.g. "A representative sample of each batch or lot of medical cannabis shall be tested by an independent cannabis testing laboratory" (§ 14.01(A)) — and a certificate of analysis must come from a testing laboratory (§ 02.08). But no testing-laboratory licence type, application section, cap, or fee exists anywhere in draft 238 NAC 1. Testing laboratories are referred to as independent contractors / sampling actors, not as a licensed establishment category. None stated None stated 0 licensed. The absence of a testing-lab licence route is, per October 2026 reporting, a central bottleneck: cultivators told the Commission there is "no viable path for testing facilities to legally process [their] product" and the Commission told growers testing guidelines "could take up to two years to finalize" — Sources: https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884965/us-ne-nebraska-s-four-licensed-cultivators-push-commission-on-testing-fees-and-physician-protections/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884587/nebraska-cannabis-cultivators-may-wait-two-years-for-testing-guidelines/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Cross-reference Part 11 (Operations Rules) for testing detail.

No other licence types exist in current regulation. In particular, there is no delivery-only licence, no research licence, no microbusiness licence, and no vertically integrated licence in the draft regulations. Delivery, if it ever occurs, would occur through the transporter certification held by a manufacturer. Any source listing a Nebraska "testing licence", "delivery licence", or "micro-licence" as current law is describing either a 2025 framework bill (LB 677's testing-facility and vertical-facility concepts — PROPOSED, indefinitely postponed) or another state's programme.

4.4 Limits on number of licences / locations — by regulation or later law

  • The only enacted later law touching licence numbers is LB 1235, which sets no licence cap. All caps are regulatory (238 NAC 1, §§ 04.12, 05.12, 06.12, summarised in §4.3).
  • Location limits beyond the caps: (a) one dispensary per judicial district (not per county or city); (b) the 1,000-foot covered-location buffer for every licence type (§4.3); (c) no licence may be issued for a premises that does not satisfy the application, zoning-compliance, and inspection requirements in the regulations — the Commission's June 2026 actions illustrate this in practice: KRL Med LLC's Washington County zoning dispute and Midwest Cultivators Group's Omaha zoning problem both blocked or relocated licensed cultivators (see §4.5). Local zoning itself is Part 3's subject and is not duplicated here, except to note that no state regulation located sets a per-city or per-county licence cap beyond the judicial-district rule for dispensaries.
  • Growth mechanism: each cap may increase by exactly one (per type statewide; per district for dispensaries) in the following year, and only if the Commission finds in a calendar year that the licensed number is insufficient to meet demand. There is no automatic escalation and no Commission finding of insufficiency has been located as of 2026-10-30.

4.5 Application timeline, statutory deadlines, and whether they were met

Statutory deadlines (Initiative 438, § 71-24,111): - Criteria / rules by 1 July 2025; begin granting registrations by 1 October 2025 (verbatim in §2.1). - Were they met? Partially, and late. - Criteria: emergency regulations were approved by the Commission on 26 June 2025 and signed by the Governor on 29 June 2025 (effective 90 days, to 28 September 2025, then extended) — secondary summaries: https://vicentellp.com/insights/cannabis-in-the-cornhusker-state-what-you-need-to-know-about-nebraska-medical-cannabis-law-and-licensing-opportunities/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("NMCC Emergency Regulations (238 Neb. Admin. Code ch. 1), effective from June 29 to September 28, 2025"); https://cannabusinessplans.com/nebraska-cannabis-market/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("On June 26, 2025, Governor Pillen signed into law emergency regulations"). On that timeline the 1 July 2025 criteria deadline was met, by days, with emergency (not permanent) regulations. - Grants by 1 October 2025: missed by one week for the first licences, and missed in substance for the programme as a whole. The first cultivator licences were offered 7 October 2025 and issued 8 October 2025 (see below), and no other licence type has been granted at all as of 2026-10-30 — more than a year after the deadline. The Commission's own minutes confirm the 8 October 2025 issuance date (see below). - Permanent regulations: approved by the Commission 13 April 2026 and signed by the Attorney General and Governor in summer 2026 (see §4.2). The April 2026 package was therefore roughly nine months after the July 2025 criteria deadline, and its fee/tracking amendment (August–October 2026) remains pending into 2027.

Cultivator round — the only completed round: - Window: 4–23 September 2025 — Source: https://lcc.nebraska.gov/medical-cannabis/medical-cannabis-commission-how-apply — accessed 2026-10-30 ("The application time period for cultivators begins September 4, 2025, and will run through September 23, 2025"). Applications were submitted by email or post under the emergency regulations approved at the 2 September 2025 meeting; no fee was charged (§2.2, Layer 2). - Volume: 39 applications received — Sources: https://news.crbmonitor.com/2025/10/nebraska-awards-the-first-two-cultivator-licenses/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("The commission received 39 cultivation applications"); https://www.doniphanherald.com/nebraska-s-first-medical-cannabis-cultivators-offered-licenses-as-legal-threats-loom/article_464b9dcb-05b0-5560-bb85-899fa1a08732.html — accessed 2026-10-30 (same figure, via Nebraska Examiner reporting). - Scoring: lottery-selected applications scored out of 100; average >70 required. First two offered 7 October 2025 to Nancy Laughlin-Wagner (Midwest Cultivators Group LLC, Omaha) and Patrick Thomas (Raymond) — scores reported as 72 and 73.33 respectively — Sources: https://beardbrospharms.com/articles/nebraska-moves-closer-to-legal-cannabis-with-first-approved-medical-marijuana-applications/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://www.doniphanherald.com/nebraska-s-first-medical-cannabis-cultivators-offered-licenses-as-legal-threats-loom/article_464b9dcb-05b0-5560-bb85-899fa1a08732.html — accessed 2026-10-30. Two other lottery-drawn applications (Crista Eggers; Stonepine Works LLC / "Sledge") were denied for scoring below 70 (Eggers average 42.33) — same sources. Commission minutes confirm: "On October 8, 2025, the Commission issued two cultivator licenses" — Source: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/MCC%20Meeting%20Minutes%203.16.26.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30; repeated in https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/April%2013%20Meeting%20Minutes.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. - Resolution of the "2 vs 4" conflict: both figures are correct at different dates. 2 is the October 2025 issuance. 4 is the total after subsequent awards in late 2025 / early 2026 brought the cap to full. Evidence for 4: the four cultivators are named together in their joint letter of 15 October 2026 — Dustin Krajewski (Meadowlark Medicinals LLC), former State Senator Kent Rogert (KRL Med LLC), Pat Thomas (MahāMotā Cultivation Co.), and Robert Wagner (Midwest Cultivators Group) — Source: https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884965/us-ne-nebraska-s-four-licensed-cultivators-push-commission-on-testing-fees-and-physician-protections/ — accessed 2026-10-30; and the Commission's 19 October 2026 meeting renewed three of the four cultivator licences (KRL Med LLC's renewal being handled separately in June 2026 for six months) — Source: https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9884587/nebraska-cannabis-cultivators-may-wait-two-years-for-testing-guidelines/ — accessed 2026-10-30. A citizen tracker citing Commission minutes records KRL Med LLC approved and Silver Ghost Solutions LLC denied at the 5 January 2026 meeting — Source: https://www.wethepeopleoftheusofa.com/taas-case-study-mcc-transparency-tracker2.html — accessed 2026-10-30 (secondary; the January minutes themselves were not opened in this session, so the 5 January date is Verify at minute level, but the 4-total is corroborated by the October 2026 primary-adjacent sources above). Any report still stating "2 cultivators" as the current total is stale (October 2025). Any report stating "4 cultivators operating" is wrong: 4 licensed, 1 growing. - Renewals: because the fee-free licences lasted 6 months, renewals fell due in spring 2026. The Commission waived sanctions for late renewal applications for 60 days from dispatch of the renewal packet (March 2026 minutes, above), and in June 2026 renewed KRL Med LLC for six more months by a 4–0 vote despite its zoning block — Source: https://mjbizdaily.com/news/nebraska-medical-marijuana-cultivator-finally-approved-to-start-growing/616598/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Renewal under the draft fee schedule would cost $23,500 + $13,000 (PROPOSED — §2.2); no renewal fee has been confirmed as actually collected. - Individual cultivator status as of mid/late 2026 (secondary, corroborated across June and October 2026 reporting): - MahāMotā Cultivation Co. (Raymond; Patrick/Pat Thomas) — inspection ratified 23 June 2026; only grower with plants in the ground; first harvest expected fall 2026. - Midwest Cultivators Group (Nancy Laughlin-Wagner / Robert Wagner) — approved to relocate from Omaha to Gretna after Omaha zoning changes; Gretna conditional-use permit approved (June 2026 reporting, above). - KRL Med LLC (former State Senator Kent Rogert) — blocked by Washington County planning/zoning reversal (county administrator held the agricultural exemption did not cover marijuana, though hemp would qualify); stop-work order under appeal; inspection scheduled 26 May 2026 did not proceed; licence renewed six months June 2026. - Meadowlark Medicinals LLC (Dustin Krajewski) — delayed its inspection (no reason given in June 2026 reporting).

Product manufacturer round — open in applications, closed in licences: - Opened: Commission voted 3–1 on 23 June 2026 to begin accepting manufacturer applications "starting Wednesday" (25 June 2026) — Sources: https://mjbizdaily.com/news/nebraska-medical-marijuana-cultivator-finally-approved-to-start-growing/616598/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30. Cap: 4. - Originally set to close 17 August 2026; extended to 14 September 2026 — Source: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/07/nebraska-medical-cannabis-commission-approves-new-licensing-fees-tracking-and-transportation-rules/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("The commission also extended its deadline for accepting medical marijuana manufacturing applications from July 20 to Aug. 17, saying it had not received a sufficient number of applications" — note this report's July 20 date conflicts with the August 17 date in later reporting; the extension sequence is therefore Verify in detail, but the September date is consistent across later sources). - Count: 10 manufacturer applications had been submitted as of the Commission's 14 September 2026 meeting — Sources: https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/nebraska-medical-marijuana-rule-updates-delayed-until-2027-as-10-manufacturer-applications-are-submitted/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://nebraskapublicmedia.org/en/news/news-articles/updated-medical-cannabis-regulations-in-limbo-until-2027/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("10 of which have been submitted so far"). - Closing-date conflict — Verify: the Marijuana Herald (15 September 2026) and Nebraska Public Media both treat 14 September 2026 (11:59 p.m. Central) as the close — Herald: "The application period officially closed September 14 at 11:59 p.m. Central Time." A later secondary licensing update claims the window "was extended a third time, to December 14, 2026, though the Medical Cannabis Commission's site still shows the outdated September 14 date" — Source: https://thecannabisbusinessadvisors.com/cannabis-licensing-update-week-of-september-21-2026/ — accessed 2026-10-30. That update itself advises confirming directly with the Commission. This report therefore states: 10 applications by 14 September 2026; whether the window closed 14 September 2026 or remains open to 14 December 2026 is Verify and must be confirmed with the Commission before advising any applicant. What is NOT in dispute: 0 manufacturer licences have been issued, and the Commission has stated it will not issue any until the fee rules are final (§2.2). For Run 1 purposes the practical answer to "are manufacturer applications open?" is: applications have been received but no licence decision is possible until the pending fee regulations are final (expected early 2027); treat the window as Verify. - No manufacturer application fee has been confirmed as collected (the draft $5,000 application fee is PROPOSED and, per the Commission's September 2026 position, fees are not yet authorised).

Dispensary round — not opened: - No application window, deadline, or application count for dispensaries has been announced by the Commission as of 2026-10-30. The Commission's "How to apply" page (August 2026 crawl) still states only that "Application periods for Dispensaries and Product Manufacturers will be set at a later date" — Source: https://lcc.nebraska.gov/medical-cannabis/medical-cannabis-commission-how-apply — accessed 2026-10-30 (stale for manufacturers, still accurate for dispensaries). Secondary reporting agrees: "The Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission has not yet announced when dispensary applications will officially open" — Source: https://thecannabisbusinessadvisors.com/nebraska-cannabis-policy-update-cultivation-inspections-begin-amid-lingering-supply-chain-gaps/ — accessed 2026-10-30; "the dispensary… licensing processes had not yet opened to applicants" (CannBus Q3 2026 — Source: https://www.cannbus.org/lsum/ne_lsum.html — accessed 2026-10-30). - Dispensary cap/awards conflict resolved: "12 reported" is the CAP (one per judicial district), not an awards figure. Awards: 0. Any source stating 12 dispensaries "awarded" or "licensed" in Nebraska is confusing the cap with issuances. The same confusion explains some "12 transporters" claims (see §4.3).

Testing laboratories — no round at all: - No application window, cap, or fee for testing laboratories exists in regulation. Testing is required but the licensing route for the tester is undefined (see §4.3). This is the single largest licensing gap in the programme and the reason cultivators cannot lawfully move harvested product even after the fall 2026 harvest.

4.6 Requirements summary (for applicants)

See §4.3 common rules. In short, an applicant must: (1) be a U.S. citizen / majority-U.S.-citizen-owned entity with 4 years' Nebraska residency (individual + officers/directors); (2) pass fingerprint-based FBI/State Patrol criminal-history checks for every person on the application, and not be a prohibited person (10-year felony/controlled-substance bar; practitioner-recommender 5-year bar; prior cannabis/cannabinoid sanction bar); (3) submit a single application for a single licence type in a window, with premises description, premises-owner identity, tax-compliance and legal-compliance statements, government photo ID with date of birth for all principals, and a business plan meeting the § 04.05 / 05.05 / 06.05 criteria; (4) score >70/100 if lottery-drawn (or if uncontested); (5) accept within 5 business days; (6) satisfy the 1,000-foot buffer and pass inspection before operating; and (7) under the draft schedule, pay the fees in §2.2 (PROPOSED). There is no minimum capital, bond, insurance, or liquid-asset threshold stated as a dollar figure in the draft regulations located — any such figure in secondary "startup cost" guides is NON-OFFICIAL and was not verified (see §4.8).

4.7 Social equity and small-business provisions

  • Enacted social-equity provisions: NONE. A full-text search of draft 238 NAC 1 for "social" returns no hits. LB 1235 as enacted contains no social-equity, disadvantaged-applicant, fee-waiver, or small-business provision. Initiatives 437/438 contain none.
  • The residency rule cuts against, not for, new/small entrants: the 4-year Nebraska residency + majority-ownership requirement, combined with the 10-year felony/controlled-substance bar (§4.3), structurally favours established, capitalised in-state operators and excludes many people with prior cannabis convictions from ownership — the opposite of a social-equity preference. Secondary analysis reaches the same conclusion: "No social equity licensing program, equity fee discount or ownership-diversity data was found for Nebraska. The main ownership rule is a residency test: 51% Nebraska ownership with four consecutive years of residency" (CannBus Market Intelligence Report Q3 2026 — Source: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ne_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-10-30).
  • A conflicting secondary claim — rejected as unsupported: CannBus's Legal Summary Q3 2026 asserts a "Statutory Social-Equity Applicant Priority Window" under which "social equity applicants were permitted to begin applying January 1, 2026, with non-social-equity applicants following April 1, 2026" — Source: https://www.cannbus.org/lsum/ne_lsum.html — accessed 2026-10-30. No statute, regulation, or Commission notice located for this research establishes any such window, any definition of "social equity applicant" in Nebraska law, or any January/April 2026 application opening tied to equity status (the only windows actually opened are cultivators September 2025 and manufacturers June 2026). Treat the January/April 2026 social-equity window claim as erroneous unless Part 1 or Part 5 locates a primary source for it. Do not repeat it.
  • PROPOSED ONLY — LB 705 (McKinney): the only social-equity proposal located. Secondary summaries describe it as reserving "over half of all licenses for social equity applicants—those from communities disproportionately affected by past cannabis-related offenses", with mentorship and financial assistance, plus the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Justice Act and Cannabis Conviction Clean Slate Act — Sources: https://beardbrospharms.com/articles/nebraska-lawmakers-propose-regulations-for-new-medical-marijuana-program/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://rss.investorbrandnetwork.com/cnw-full/420-with-cnw-nebraskan-lawmakers-consider-proposals-to-implement-voter-approved-medical-marijuana-program/ — accessed 2026-10-30. LB 705 was indefinitely postponed 17 April 2026 (§2.7). It is PROPOSED ONLY and creates no current right, preference, or programme.
  • Small-business provisions: NONE enacted. No micro-licence, tiered licence by size, fee tier by revenue, or small-business set-aside exists in current regulation. LB 705's tiered business sizes (micro/small/large, per secondary summaries) were likewise PROPOSED ONLY and postponed with the bill. The draft regulations' only size-related provisions are the 1,250-flowering-plant cap (cultivators) and the licence caps themselves, which apply equally to all applicants.

4.8 Official information pages and estimated startup costs

Official information pages (opened or returned in search for this research): - Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission — regulations (draft 21 Aug 2026, Title 238 Ch. 1): https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/Revised%20-%20238%20NAC%201%20DRAFT.8.21.final.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. - Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission — meeting minutes (April 13 2026; March 16 2026): https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/April%2013%20Meeting%20Minutes.pdf and https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/MCC%20Meeting%20Minutes%203.16.26.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. (Part 5 must inventory the full minutes/meeting-information page; the Commission's meeting-information page itself was not opened in this session.) - Nebraska Liquor Control Commission — Medical Cannabis Commission "How to apply" (application form; criminal-history request forms; State Patrol fingerprint instructions): https://lcc.nebraska.gov/medical-cannabis/medical-cannabis-commission-how-apply — accessed 2026-10-30. Note: this page is stale (August 2026 crawl; still describes the September 2025 cultivator window and September 2025 emergency regulations). The Commission has a standalone mcc.nebraska.gov site; applicants should confirm current instructions there before filing, particularly the manufacturer-window close date (§4.5). - Registered Cannabis Establishment Application Form (PDF, via mcc.nebraska.gov): https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/APPLICATION%20FORM_1.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30 (returned in search; form lists licence types as "Cultivator / Manufacturer / Dispensary / Transporter" checkboxes — the "Transporter" checkbox on the old form predates the draft's certification-only model and must not be read as a standalone transporter licence; see §4.3). - Application submission (from the "How to apply" page): by email to the Commission's public address or by U.S. Postal Service to PO Box 95046, Lincoln, NE 68509, "consistent with the current emergency regulations" — same source as above. No online portal was confirmed as operational in sources located; the draft regulations contemplate submission "through an electronic application portal designated by the Commission" (§ 03.03) as an alternative, but no portal URL was located — Verify. - Nebraska Legislature — statutes: §§ 71-24,103–71-24,111 (Medical Cannabis Acts); § 71-24,111 (Commission powers/deadlines): https://www.nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=71-24,111&print=true — accessed 2026-10-30. Bill pages: LB 1235 slip law, LB 651, LB 677, LB 705 (see §2.7 links).

Estimated startup costs: - No official startup-cost estimate exists. The Commission publishes no build-out, equipment, security, testing, insurance, or working-capital estimate. - NON-OFFICIAL ESTIMATE — fee stack only (derived arithmetic from the PROPOSED draft schedule, §2.2): first-year state fees of $38,000 (cultivator or manufacturer without transport certification), $48,000 (manufacturer with certification), or $33,000 (dispensary), plus $13,000 inventory-tracking fee already included in those totals, and annual renewal stacks of $36,500 / $41,500 / $31,000 respectively. These are fee totals only. No total startup cost (premises, greenhouse/indoor build, extraction equipment, security systems, testing, labour, inventory, legal/compliance) can be stated from any source located, official or reputable secondary, and none is estimated here. Secondary "how to open a dispensary" guides found in search (e.g. a "two-year license fee (estimate $50–75k)" claim) cite no Commission source and conflict with the draft fee schedule; they are not relied on. Any future startup-cost figure must be labelled NON-OFFICIAL ESTIMATE with its source and must separate state fees (above) from private build-out costs. - One cost actually incurred by the first cultivators that IS documented: the 6-month, fee-free licence cycle forced repeat renewals and, for KRL Med LLC and others, zoning/legal costs from county disputes — but no dollar figure for those costs was located (Unavailable).

4.9 Adult-use licence types — PROPOSED ONLY (none in law)

  • There are no adult-use licence types in Nebraska law. Initiatives 437/438 are medical-only. No adult-use bill was enacted or is pending as a licensing bill in the 109th Legislature.
  • PROPOSED / FAILED TO QUALIFY — 2026 citizen constitutional initiative: filed (August 2025) by Bill Hawkins (Nebraska Hemp Company), proposing a new constitutional section: "All persons twenty-one years of age or older have the right to use all plants in the genus Cannabis." It required signatures from 10% of registered voters (roughly 125,000) by 3 July 2026, including 5% in each of two-fifths of Nebraska's 93 counties. It did not qualify for the 3 November 2026 ballot — Sources: https://www.marijuanamoment.net/new-nebraska-measure-to-create-a-constitutional-right-to-use-marijuana-filed-for-2026-ballot/ — accessed 2026-10-30 (filing and signature requirements); https://drugpolicywatch.info/states/nebraska.html — accessed 2026-10-30 ("A 2026 citizen initiative to create a constitutional right to use cannabis went through signature gathering against a July 3, 2026 deadline and did not make the ballot"); https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/the-states-most-likely-to-legalize-adult-use-cannabis-over-the-next-couple-years/ — accessed 2026-10-30 ("Supporters also filed an adult-use legalization initiative for the 2026 election cycle, although it did not qualify for the ballot"). It contained no licence types in any event — it was a use right, not a licensing scheme — and must never be listed as an adult-use licence proposal with cultivator/dispensary categories.
  • LR 456 (Dungan, 109th Leg., Second Session, 2026) — interim study only: "to examine the implementation of recreational cannabis in Nebraska", including regulatory, economic, and patient-access impacts, with the General Affairs Committee to report findings/recommendations to the Legislative Council or Legislature — Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/FloorDocs/109/PDF/Intro/LR456.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. An interim study resolution creates no licence, no right, and no programme. Listed here only so it is not mistaken for an adult-use licensing bill.
  • Consequence for Part 4: the adult-use licence table is empty by law. Any "adult-use licence" mentioned in a Nebraska business plan as of 2026-10-30 is hypothetical.

4.10 Cross-reference — hemp licences (full detail in Run 2, Part 7)

  • Hemp licensing sits under the Nebraska Hemp Farming Act (Neb. Rev. Stat. §§ 2-501–2-518) administered by the Nebraska Department of Agriculture, not the Medical Cannabis Commission. Licence categories, fees, and counts are NOT detailed here by design (Run 2, Part 7).
  • The only hemp-licence fact needed for Part 4 coherence: the medical programme's cultivator application and reporting repeatedly intersect with hemp licensing in practice (e.g. Patrick Thomas, the first cultivator licensee, was reported as holding a USDA hemp licence; KRL Med LLC was told by Washington County that hemp would qualify for the agricultural exemption that marijuana did not — Sources: https://news.crbmonitor.com/2025/10/nebraska-awards-the-first-two-cultivator-licenses/ — accessed 2026-10-30; https://mjbizdaily.com/news/nebraska-medical-marijuana-cultivator-finally-approved-to-start-growing/616598/ — accessed 2026-10-30). A hemp licence is not a medical cannabis licence and confers no right to cultivate, manufacture, or dispense medical cannabis, and vice versa.

Part 5 — Licence Data Sources

NEBRASKA — Run 1, PART 5: LICENCE DATA SOURCES (inventory only)

Scope note — read first. This part does NOT list every licence holder. It inventories where licence-holder data lives, in what form, and what it does and does not contain. Where a source is a meeting minute, a press release or a news report rather than a registry, that is stated plainly. Every fact below carries its source link and the access date 2026-10-30.

Headline finding. As of 2026-10-30 there is no single, public, downloadable Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission (MCC) licensee register located. Medical-cannabis licensee information has to be reconstructed from MCC meeting minutes, agendas and adopted regulations — there is no MCC search page, dashboard or CSV/XLSX list found. Hemp is the opposite: the historical Nebraska state programme published annual-summary PDFs with counts (to 2024), and current hemp-producer licensing is federal (USDA), searchable only through USDA's public tool. The Attorney General's hemp-retailer enforcement has no public retailer list or CSV either — only press releases with aggregate counts. [All sources accessed 2026-10-30]
