5.1 — Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission: meeting information (the de facto licensee record)

  • URL: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/meeting-information — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Holder type: Medical cannabis establishments (cultivator, product manufacturer, dispensary, transporter) — only insofar as a licensing vote, renewal, relocation or inspection ratification is minuted.
  • Format: Interactive calendar/list page (HTML) linking to individual PDF minutes, draft minutes, agendas and Zoom recordings. This is not a dashboard and not a searchable licensee database; there is no download button for a consolidated list and no export function on the page as fetched.
  • How to access: Open the page; each dated entry (e.g. Dec 14, Nov 16, Oct 19, Oct 15 public hearing, Sep 14, Aug 17, Jul 20, Jun 22, May 11, Apr 13, Mar 16, Feb 02, Jan 05 in 2026; Dec 02, Nov 03, Oct 07, Sep 30, Sep 08 emergency, Sep 02, Aug 04, Jun 26, Jun 09 inaugural in 2025) opens that meeting's documents. The page text fetched 2026-10-30 lists dates/times/rooms (301 Centennial Mall South, 1st Floor Hearing Room, Lincoln, NE 68509) but the minutes links themselves sit behind the per-meeting entries — click through per meeting. Source: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/meeting-information — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Update frequency as stated: None stated on the page. In practice, entries are added per meeting; some minutes remain marked "DRAFT" / "not approved" months later (see below) — Verify approval status on each PDF before citing it as final.
  • Latest data date: The page as fetched lists a Dec 14 public meeting (year not shown in the text extract; context is the 2026 calendar) and, in the search-indexed record, a Sep 14, 2026 draft-minutes document. The most recent approved minutes cannot be confirmed from the page extract alone — Verify.
  • Fields available: Meeting date, time, location; agenda items (including "new cultivator application evaluations and approval of offers/denials of licenses" — e.g. the Mar 16, 2026 agenda); minutes narrative naming applicants/licensees voted on, vote counts, renewals (e.g. a six-month renewal), relocations and inspection ratifications; Zoom recording links/passcodes for some meetings.
  • Limitations:
  • No consolidated licensee table, no licence numbers, no addresses in a single place, no search by holder name, no CSV/XLSX export.
  • Coverage is uneven: a secondary audit of this page (Sep–Oct 2026) found some meetings with minutes attached, most without, and some minutes still marked draft months later — https://www.wethepeopleoftheusofa.com/taas-case-study-mcc-transparency-tracker2.html — accessed 2026-10-30 (secondary; use only to flag the gap, not as a substitute for the MCC page).
  • Older (LCC-hosted) minutes live on a different domain — see §5.4.
  • Direct downloadable files located (individual minutes, not a register):
  • Medical Cannabis Commission Meeting Minutes September 2 2025.pdf — https://lcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/medical_cannabis/Medical%20Cannabis%20Commission%20Meeting%20Minutes%20September%202%202025.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • MCC Meeting Minutes 10.7.25.pdf — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/MCC%20Meeting%20Minutes%2010.7.25.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30 (the Oct 7, 2025 meeting at which the first two cultivator licences were offered, per secondary reporting — Verify names/votes in the PDF itself before republication)
  • September 14, 2026 DRAFT Meeting Minutes.pdf — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/September%2014%2C%202026%20DRAFT%20Meeting%20Minutes.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30 — expressly marked "These minutes have not been approved by the commission."
  • Meeting Minutes August 17 2026.pdf — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/Meeting%20Minutes%20August%2017%202026.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30 (contains the Gretna conditional-use-permit update for a cultivator relocation — see §5.11)
  • Medical Cannabis Commission Meeting Mintutes May 11 2026.pdf [sic — "Mintutes" in the source URL] — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/Medical%20Cannabis%20Commission%20Meeting%20Mintutes%20May%2011%202026.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • March Draft Agenda.pdf (Mar 16, 2026 agenda) — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/March%20Draft%20Agenda.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • No other MCC minutes PDF URLs were exposed verbatim in the text fetches for this inventory. Do not guess further filenames; retrieve them from https://mcc.nebraska.gov/meeting-information — accessed 2026-10-30, meeting by meeting.

5.2 — MCC "How to apply" page (application intake, NOT a licensee list)

  • URL: https://lcc.nebraska.gov/medical-cannabis/medical-cannabis-commission-how-apply — accessed 2026-10-30 (page copy crawled 2026-08-24; content is the Sep 2025 cultivator window and is now stale for later licence types)
  • Holder type: Prospective medical-cannabis applicants (cultivator first; dispensary and manufacturer periods "set at a later date" on this page).
  • Format: HTML information page + linked PDF application form and Nebraska State Patrol fingerprint forms. No search, no dashboard, no download of applicant/licensee data.
  • How to access/download: Applications were to be sent by email or US mail to PO Box 95046, Lincoln, NE 68509; the page states "There is currently no fee to submit an application" (2025 cultivator window) and that the cultivator window ran September 4–23, 2025. The linked form is labelled on the page only as "APPLICATION FORM" and "Criminal History Record Request - English-Fillable.pdf" / "- Spanish Fillable.pdf" — the exact application-form URL was not exposed in the text fetch, so no direct download link is asserted here — Verify by opening the page and clicking "APPLICATION FORM". Source: https://lcc.nebraska.gov/medical-cannabis/medical-cannabis-commission-how-apply — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Update frequency / latest data date: Not stated on the page. The page still describes the Sep 2025 cultivator window, while the MCC rules page (§5.3) describes a new manufacturer period opening in early 2027 — the "How to apply" page is therefore out of date as a current-applications source and must not be used for 2026–27 manufacturer/dispensary status.
  • Fields available: None for existing holders. It gives only intake instructions, residency rule text (§005.03: majority owned by US citizens resident in Nebraska ≥4 years; >51% financial/voting interests), and the $30.00 State Patrol fingerprint fee note.
  • Limitations: It publishes no applicant list, no scores, no lottery results and no licence numbers. The 39 cultivator applications received Sep 4–23, 2025 (secondary reporting) are not on this page — Verify against MCC minutes if a total applicant count is needed.

5.3 — MCC adopted regulations and proposed amendments (licence caps and fee schedule, not a holder list)

  • URL: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/adopted-rules-regulations — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Holder type: All medical-cannabis establishment types (cultivator, manufacturer, dispensary, transporter) — rules only.
  • Format: HTML page linking to PDFs: "Adopted Regulations.pdf" (current permanent regulations) and "Proposed Amendments to the Regulations" (moved forward Aug 17, 2026) plus a "Formal Rulemaking Process.jpg" flowchart. No dashboard, no holder search, no CSV.
  • How to access/download: Open the page and click the PDF links. The exact PDF URLs were not exposed in the text fetch — no direct download link is asserted here — Verify by opening the page. Do not construct a sites/default/files/… URL by guesswork.
  • Update frequency / latest data date: Page states: current permanent regulations were approved by Attorney General Mike Hilgers on June 30, 2026 and by Governor Jim Pillen on July 1, 2026; the proposed amendments were voted forward Aug 17, 2026, with a public hearing Oct 15, 2026, Commission consideration scheduled Nov 16, 2026, and new regulations + a new manufacturer application period anticipated early 2027. Until then the Commission "will continue operating under the current licensing and renewal structure, including the current six-month renewal schedule." Source: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/adopted-rules-regulations — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Fields available: Licence caps and structure (the page states the Commission "may select up to four manufacturers"), fee-schedule proposal, renewal structure. No holder names, addresses or licence numbers.
  • Limitations: The proposed-amendments PDF is a proposal, not current law and not a licence list. A separate secondary summary gives a draft fee figure ($5,000 application fee; cultivator renewal $23,500 + $13,000 tracking fee = $36,500/year in the proposal) — those figures are secondary and Verify against the proposed-amendments PDF itself; they are not repeated here as official fees.

5.4 — Nebraska Liquor Control Commission (LCC) legacy MCC pages

  • URL pattern: https://lcc.nebraska.gov — accessed 2026-10-30; example documents:
  • MCC press release, Nov 18, 2025 (manufacturer listening session, Dec 2, 2025) — MCC Press Release 11.18.25.pdf — https://lcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/MCC%20Press%20Release%2011.18.25.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • LCC-hosted MCC agenda, Sep 8, 2025 emergency meeting — https://lcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/doc/MCC%20Agenda%20September%208%2C%202025.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Holder type: Same as §5.1 (historical MCC material while the Commission was supported by LCC).
  • Format: PDF agendas/minutes/press releases. No search, no dashboard, no consolidated download.
  • Limitations: The LCC site is a legacy host; current MCC material is at mcc.nebraska.gov. Any pre-2026 licensee fact found only on lcc.nebraska.gov should be cross-checked against the MCC minutes for the same date. The Commission's move toward operating independently of LCC (ending the temporary office/staffing agreement) is reported secondarily — Verify before describing LCC's current role.

5.5 — Patient and practitioner statistics: NO source located

  • No MCC patient registry, practitioner directory or statistics page was located. Secondary sources agree on the absence: MPP's patient-numbers table lists Nebraska as N/A / N/A — https://www.mpp.org/issues/medical-marijuana/state-by-state-medical-marijuana-laws/medical-marijuana-patient-numbers/ — accessed 2026-10-30; a secondary MCC-transparency audit states "there is still no patient registry, and no published count of how many practitioners have written a recommendation" — https://www.wethepeopleoftheusofa.com/taas-case-study-mcc-transparency-tracker2.html — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • What this means for Part 5: Patient counts, practitioner counts and recommendation counts are Unavailable, not zero. Do not infer a count from the absence of a registry. The introduced-then-removed registry in LB 1235 (as introduced) is a legislative-history point for Part 1/Part 4, not a data source; its final status must be confirmed on nebraskalegislature.gov before any registry claim is repeated.
  • Related non-source: The Nebraska Department of Health and Human Services (DHHS) publishes no medical-cannabis patient statistics page located in this search — Unavailable / Verify by direct DHHS-site check in Run 2 if a DHHS source is to be claimed.

5.6 — Nebraska Department of Agriculture (NDA): hemp programme — HISTORICAL state lists (closed)

  • Landing URL (opened directly): https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Holder type (historical): Hemp cultivators, processor-handlers and brokers licensed by NDA under the Nebraska Hemp Farming Act.
  • Format: HTML landing page linking to PDF annual summaries only — 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024. There is no searchable NDA licensee database, no CSV/XLSX, no per-holder list on this page as fetched. The summaries give aggregate counts, planted/harvested acreage and disposal lots — not holder names.
  • How to access/download: Open https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp — accessed 2026-10-30 and click each year's summary link. Exact direct links confirmed by opening/fetching:
  • 2024: file name 2024 NDA Hemp Program Annual Summary.pdf — https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp/2024%20NDA%20Hemp%20Program%20Annual%20Summary.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • 2023: file name 2023 NDA Hemp Program Annual Summary.pdf — https://nda.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/hemp/2023%20NDA%20Hemp%20Program%20Annual%20Summary.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • 2021: file name 2021NDAHempProgramAnnualSummary.pdf — https://Nda.nebraska.gov/hemp/2021NDAHempProgramAnnualSummary.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30
  • 2019, 2020, 2022: linked from the same landing page (links 1, 2 and 4 in the page fetch) but their exact filenames/URLs were not exposed in the text fetch — Verify by clicking through from https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp — accessed 2026-10-30; do not guess filenames.
  • Update frequency / latest data date: Page footer: "Updated January 2025." No update after the programme closed is shown. The 2024 summary is therefore the final NDA annual summary.
  • Fields available (per summary): Licence counts by type; licensed / planted / harvested indoor sq ft and outdoor acres; cultivators by hemp type (cannabinoid/flower, fiber, grain, multiple); lots/acres destroyed for non-compliance (2024: 0 lots, 0 acres).
  • Limitations: Counts only — no holder names, no site locations, no licence numbers in the summaries as fetched. A pre-2025 "list of licensed hemp cultivators" is referenced in secondary histories but no such list file was located on the current NDA page — treat any claim of a downloadable NDA holder list as Unavailable unless the Run 2 researcher retrieves it from an archive.

5.6a — THE HEMP TRANSITION (verified on official pages — state precisely)

  • What happened, on official sources: The NDA landing page itself now directs Nebraska cultivators to USDA or a tribal programme and states Nebraska applicants could submit a USDA application from fall 2024 "to receive a USDA hemp producer license on January 1, 2025" — https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp — accessed 2026-10-30. The 2024 summary states verbatim in substance: "Legislation was enacted that will change how hemp is regulated in Nebraska. LB 262 of 2024 cedes regulatory authority of hemp cultivation to the United States Department of Agriculture (USDA). The Nebraska Department of Agriculture Hemp Program will cease to exist beginning January 1, 2025." — https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp/2024%20NDA%20Hemp%20Program%20Annual%20Summary.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Legislative corroboration: The Legislature's own session review states provisions of LB 999 (carried in LB 262) "transfer licensure and regulation of hemp cultivation from the state Department of Agriculture to the U.S. Department of Agriculture, beginning Jan. 1, 2025," terminate the state Hemp Commission and Hemp Promotion Fund, and that "The state will continue to regulate hemp transportation within Nebraska" — https://update.legislature.ne.gov/?p=36594 — accessed 2026-10-30 and https://update.legislature.ne.gov/?p=36361 — accessed 2026-10-30 (LB 262 passed 47–0, Apr 11, 2024, per the latter).
  • What this means for NDA licence lists after that date: 1. No new NDA cultivator, processor-handler or broker licences exist after 31 Dec 2024. All NDA processor-handler and broker licences expired 31 Dec 2024 and were not renewed (secondary summary of the same transition — Verify the "not renewed" wording against LB 262 text before republication, but the expiry follows from the programme ceasing to exist Jan 1, 2025, per the official 2024 summary). 2. NDA lists are therefore frozen historical records (2019–2024 summaries only). Any "current NDA hemp licensee list" dated 2025 or 2026 would be wrong. 3. Current Nebraska hemp-producer licensing is USDA-only (plus tribal programmes for tribal land — see §5.7/§5.10). Processor-handler and broker licensing at state level has no successor programme identified on the NDA page — the NDA page gives no post-2024 processor/handler route; that gap is Verify/Unavailable for Part 7, not filled here. 4. Nebraska does not run its own USDA-approved state plan today: USDA lists Nebraska as "USDA Hemp Producer License" (see §5.7).

5.7 — USDA Agricultural Marketing Service (AMS): current hemp-producer records for Nebraska

  • Plan-status page (opened directly): https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Content verified: Page updated February 17, 2026; descriptor: "USDA Hemp Producer License: USDA will issue the producer license"; table row: Nebraska — USDA Hemp Producer License. Tribal rows on the same page: Iowa Tribe of Kansas and Nebraska — Approved Plan (pdf); Omaha Tribe of Nebraska — Approved Plan (pdf)*; Santee Sioux Nation — Approved Plan (pdf); Winnebago Tribe of Nebraska — Approved Plan (pdf)*. Source: https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Format: HTML table with PDF links for approved plans. No holder list, no CSV, no Nebraska count on this page.
  • USDA Hemp Public Search Tool (interactive search, no download confirmed):
  • URL (as cited by USDA-linked sources): https://hemp.ams.usda.gov/s/PublicSearchTool — accessed 2026-10-30. This URL was returned verbatim in USDA-related search results (Kansas portal and a state-agency bulletin citing USDA's tool) — it was not opened as a live interactive session in this text-only inventory, so its current fields, filters and any export button are Verify by a live check.
  • Holder type: USDA-licensed hemp producers (including Nebraska producers licensed directly by USDA since Jan 1, 2025) plus producers reported by states/tribes with approved plans.
  • Format: Interactive search page (HTML/JS). No downloadable file is offered on the pages opened; whether the tool has an export/download button is Verify — do not assert one.
  • How to access: Open the tool, filter/search by state (Nebraska) and/or entity name / licence number. USDA's FAQ states: "Information on licensees in your area can be obtained through the Hemp Public Search Tool" — https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/frequently-asked-questions-faq — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Update frequency / latest data date: Not stated on the pages opened — Verify. The tool is populated from state/tribal reports to USDA plus USDA's own licences; completeness therefore depends on reporting lag.
  • Fields available: Not confirmed from a live session — Verify. Do not assert field names (licence number, county, lot, etc.) without opening the tool.
  • Limitations: Search-only; no Nebraska aggregate total is published on the tool's landing material; producers under tribal approved plans appear under the tribal programme, not under "Nebraska" state licensing, and must be searched separately.
  • USDA Hemp eManagement Platform (HeMP) — licence-holder system (restricted):
  • URL: https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/hemp-emanagement-platform-hemp — accessed 2026-10-30 (HeMP information page; login requires USDA eAuthentication or Login.gov).
  • Format: Secure account system, not a public search. Licence holders manage their own licence/reporting there.
  • Law-enforcement search: A separate HeMP law-enforcement tool exists at https://hempsearch.ams.usda.gov (per USDA's Law Enforcement Search Tool User Guide PDF — https://ams-prod.azureedge.us/sites/default/files/media/Law_Enforcement_Search_Tool_User_Guide.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30), requiring eAuth + USDA-approved law-enforcement access requested via farmbill.hemp@usda.gov. Not a public licensee source.
  • USDA FOIA-released records: USDA's FAQ notes records previously released under FOIA are available (sentence truncated in the fetch) — Verify the FOIA library URL and whether a Nebraska extract exists; no FOIA file is listed here because none was opened.
  • USDA grower information page: https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/information-for-hemp-growers — accessed 2026-10-30 (confirms: if a state/tribe has no approved plan, apply for a USDA licence via HeMP; applications accepted on a rolling basis).

5.8 — Attorney General: hemp-retailer enforcement and lawsuit filings (no retailer register)

  • News index (browse, no data file): https://ago.nebraska.gov/archived-news — accessed 2026-10-30. Format: HTML monthly news archive. No search-by-defendant, no CSV, no export button found. Use it to date releases (e.g. "August 01, 2025 — Attorney General Mike Hilgers Sues Savage Enterprises…"; "April 17, 2025 — …Expansion of Synthetic Delta-8 Fight to Nebraska City"; "April 03, 2025 — …Expansion … to Grand Island and Kearney").
  • Omaha expansion release (opened directly): https://ago.nebraska.gov/news/announcement-new-developments-delta-8-enforcement-efforts — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Content: Cease-and-desist letters "relating to 104 retail locations and franchised stores in Omaha"; 2025 Douglas County Sheriff / AG purchases from stores owned by 35 different companies; each investigated store had at least one product marketed as hemp with delta-9 over the legal limit and mislabelled contents; stores asked to sign an Assurance of Voluntary Compliance or face litigation; "To date, the Attorney General's Office has filed 15 lawsuits" (Consumer Protection Act unfairness + deception, Nebraska Pure Food Act), 12 of 15 settled, 3 actively litigated.
  • Fields available: Aggregate counts + city (Omaha) + company count (35) + example product names (Kushy Exotic THCA Flower, Flying Horse Vape-9G, Road Trip Gummies). No retailer names, no store addresses, no defendant list, no case numbers in the release.
  • Savage Enterprises release (opened directly): https://ago.nebraska.gov/news/attorney-general-mike-hilgers-sues-savage-enterprises-violating-nebraskas-consumer-protection — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Content: Lawsuit against Savage Enterprises (Wyoming corporation, based in California) for THC gummies/vapes, youth-targeted branding, mislabelled THC forms/concentrations; cease-and-desist letters "from Ralston to Scottsbluff" affecting franchised-store locations in Kimball, Lexington, McCook, Rushville, Ralston, Scottsbluff, Seward, Sidney, and South Sioux City, Nebraska; "To date, the Attorney General's Office has sent 225 cease and desist letters and filed 16 lawsuits" (same three statutory theories).
  • Court / case number: The AG release itself gives no court and no case number. Secondary reporting places the Savage suit in Lancaster County District Court — https://www.marijuanamoment.net/nebraska-attorney-general-expands-intoxicating-hemp-and-delta-8-crackdown/ — accessed 2026-10-30 — but the case number is Verify/Unavailable from the sources opened; obtain it from the Lancaster County District Court / Nebraska JUSTICE case record before citing it.
  • Conflict to carry into research notes: 15 lawsuits (Omaha release) vs 16 lawsuits (Savage release, Aug 1, 2025 per the AG archive). The Savage release is the later-dated filing (16th suit), which reconciles the direction of travel, but the Omaha release's own date is not shown in its text fetch — Verify both release dates before presenting a single "lawsuits filed" total. Likewise "225 letters" (Savage release) is the latest aggregate located; earlier secondary summaries report "200+" and "204" at earlier points in 2025 — those are superseded snapshots, not competing totals, provided dates are attached.
  • Format / download: Press releases are HTML pages with, in the Savage case, a court-filing attachment noted ("Exploitative images … redacted in court filing below") — the filing's direct PDF URL was not exposed in the text fetch — Verify by opening the release page. There is no AG enforcement database, CSV or retailer list.

5.9 — Nebraska Secretary of State: business-entity search (indirect holder identification only)

  • Corporate and Business landing page (opened directly): https://sos.nebraska.gov/business-services/corporate-and-business — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Holder type: All Nebraska-registered entities (corporations, LLCs, limited partnerships, LLPs, trade names, trademarks, service marks) — it is not a cannabis/hemp licence register and has no cannabis filter, no licence-type field and no licence-status field.
  • Format: Interactive search page (free) + paid subscriber access + paid special-request data files. The free search has no bulk download and no export button stated on the landing page.
  • How to access: From the landing page, click "Corporation and Business Search" — described on the official page as: "A free search of the Corporate and Business database for information on all entities, trade names, trademarks, and service marks. From here you can also use a credit card to order Certificates of Good Standing and copies of filed documents." Source: https://sos.nebraska.gov/business-services/corporate-and-business — accessed 2026-10-30
  • Search tool URL — Verify: Secondary guides give the direct tool as https://www.nebraska.gov/sos/corp/corpsearch.cgi?nav=search — accessed 2026-10-30 (secondary source, not confirmed in the official page's text fetch, whose search link target resolved only to www.nebraska.gov). Verify the exact search URL by clicking through from the official landing page before publishing it.
  • Search modes / fields (secondary, consistent across guides — Verify against the live tool): Name Starts With / Keyword / Sounds Like / Exact Match (≥3 characters) or search by SOS Account Number; results show entity name, account number, type and status; detail page shows registered agent name/address, status (e.g. Active/Dissolved) and scanned formation/biennial-report filings. The tool does not show members/managers beyond the registered agent, financials, tax status or cannabis-licence status.
  • Bulk / downloadable option (official, paid): "Corporate Searches - Special Requests — Order corporate records by: entity type, date registered or incorporated, keyword(s) in the name, and/or location. Records search for registered agents in a specific location are also available. Data files are provided for immediate download in CSV format. Cost for record batches is $15/1,000 records." Source: https://sos.nebraska.gov/business-services/corporate-and-business — accessed 2026-10-30. This is the only SOS bulk-download route located; it is a paid, keyword-filtered extract, not a free cannabis list, and no cannabis-keyword extract file is published.
  • Update frequency / latest data date: Not stated on the landing page — the database is the live corporate register, updated on filing. Verify any "as of" date by running the search on the day of use.
  • Limitations: Useful only to confirm that a named MCC licensee entity (e.g. a cultivator LLC named in minutes) exists as a Nebraska entity and who its registered agent is. It cannot generate a list of cannabis licensees, because licence status is not a field. A new online business filing system launching December 7, 2026 is reported secondarily — Verify against the SOS site before the Run 2 update, as it may change the search URL.
  • Business Services Division contact page (opened via search): https://sos.nebraska.gov/business-services/business-services-division — accessed 2026-10-30 (registrations recorded for corporations, LLCs, LPs, LLPs, trade names and trademarks; corporate phone (402) 471-4079).

5.11 — Local (county / city / village) permit records where published

  • Finding: no central or searchable local cannabis-permit database was located for Nebraska. Local permission appears only as (a) a condition noted in MCC minutes, (b) a city council/commission action reported in news, or (c) a county zoning decision contested in an appeal — none of which is published as a permit list.
  • Gretna (Sarpy County) — conditional-use permit (the one local permit positively evidenced):
  • MCC Aug 17, 2026 minutes (via search-indexed extract) state a cultivator "has taken possession of its new facility following the Commission's approval of its address change in June and the City of Gretna's approval of its Conditional Use Permit" — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/Meeting%20Minutes%20August%2017%202026.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. Corroborated secondarily: "Gretna leaders already approved a requested conditional-use permit" — https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • No Gretna online permit portal or permit document was located — the permit itself (number, date, conditions) is Verify via a Gretna city records request / council minutes; the MCC minutes are the only source located for its existence.
  • Omaha (Douglas County) — zoning change, no permit list:
  • A cultivator's Omaha site was abandoned after "updated zoning laws for medical cannabis through the city of Omaha" and a conditional-use-permit application to Omaha, before relocation to Gretna (May 11, 2026 MCC minutes extract) — https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/Medical%20Cannabis%20Commission%20Meeting%20Mintutes%20May%2011%202026.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. No Omaha permit database or ordinance list was located in this search — Verify via Omaha planning/permits records in Part 3.
  • Washington County — zoning reversal / stop-work, no permit list:
  • A cultivator reported a Washington County planning/zoning administrator reversal on an agricultural exemption ("Hemp … would qualify, but not marijuana"), a stop-work order and a local appeal (Apr 13, 2026 MCC meeting reporting) — https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30. No Washington County online permit record was located — Verify via county planning records.
  • Other jurisdictions checked by search with no published cannabis-permit list located: Lincoln/Lancaster, Bellevue/Papillion (Sarpy), Grand Island/Hall, Kearney/Buffalo, Fremont/Dodge, Norfolk/Madison, Hastings/Adams, North Platte/Lincoln County, Columbus/Platte, Scottsbluff, South Sioux City/Dakota, Beatrice/Gage. Absence of a located list is not proof no local rule exists — Part 3 must check each jurisdiction's code/minutes individually; for Part 5 the correct statement is: no published local cannabis-permit register was found for any of these jurisdictions as of 2026-10-30 — Unavailable.
  • Tobacco licences as a proxy — do not use: The AG notes many investigated hemp retailers "have tobacco licenses" which "may put their tobacco licenses at risk" — https://ago.nebraska.gov/news/announcement-new-developments-delta-8-enforcement-efforts — accessed 2026-10-30. Nebraska tobacco-licence records (Department of Revenue / local) are not a hemp or cannabis licence list and are not inventoried as one here; whether a tobacco-licence search is public is Verify if Part 5's successor wants it as a cross-reference.

Part 6 — Market Data

PART 6 — MARKET DATA

6.1 Medical sales, licensees and patients — the headline is zero sales

  • Medical cannabis sales in Nebraska: $0 / none. "Nebraska has no legal cannabis sales yet. No dispensary or product manufacturer is licensed, and no patient has been registered. Sales and trajectory figures are N/A." — CannBus, Nebraska Cannabis Market Intelligence Report Q3 2026, data as of 30 Sep 2026 (secondary, modelled/compiled) — accessed 2026-10-30. This is a secondary compilation, but it is consistent with every official-source signal below (no dispensary licence, no manufacturer licence, first cultivator only cleared to plant in June 2026).
  • Dispensary licences issued: 0 (cap 12, one per judicial district). — CannBus Q3 2026 Market Report, data as of 30 Sep 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30. Corroborated by the Marijuana Policy Project implementation timeline: "Dispensaries are not yet licensed as of Jan. 2, 2026" and "The first cultivation centers were licensed in October 2025." — Marijuana Policy Project, Medical Cannabis Program Implementation Timelines — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Cultivator licences issued: 4 (the state cap). The Commission's June 2026 meeting dealt with all four: MahāMotā (cleared to plant), KRL Med (renewed 6 months, zoning-disputed), Midwest Cultivator Group (relocation approved), Meadowlark Medicinals (inspection delayed). — MJBizDaily, 23 June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30; and North Platte Post, June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Product-manufacturer licences issued: 0 as of the June–September 2026 reports. The Commission voted 3–1 on 22 June 2026 to begin accepting manufacturer applications (up to 4 licences under the regulations); by 14 Sep 2026 ten manufacturer applications had been submitted, and the Commission said it would not license manufacturers or dispensaries until fees for those licences were authorised. — CannBus Q3 2026 Market Report, data as of 30 Sep 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30; and MJBizDaily, 23 June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify the manufacturer-licensing outcome after September 2026 — the application window/fee status is the main moving item.
  • Transporter licences issued: Verify / none reported. The regulations allow up to 12 transporters, but no transporter licence issuance was reported in the sources opened. — MJBizDaily, 23 June 2026 (caps stated) — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Registered patients: none / N/A — there is no patient registry to count. "Registered patients: None: no patient registrations had been established as of mid-2026." — CannBus Q3 2026 Market Report — accessed 2026-10-30. The Marijuana Policy Project patient-numbers table lists Nebraska as "N/A / N/A." — Marijuana Policy Project, Medical Cannabis Patient Numbers — accessed 2026-10-30. MPP's timeline notes "there is no registry, patients can possess cannabis with a certification." — Marijuana Policy Project, Implementation Timelines — accessed 2026-10-30. Note for Part 1/Part 4: whether LB1235 (approved by the Governor 7 Apr 2026) ultimately created, removed or left out a patient registry is contested in secondary coverage and is Verify against the enrolled LB1235 text — do not publish a patient count, and do not publish a registry fee, until that is settled.
  • Earliest patient sales: spring 2027 (tentative, secondary — not an official date). "Though the first harvest could be ready by the fall, regulators haven't updated a tentative date of spring 2027 for the first sale." — MJBizDaily, 23 June 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30. CannBus likewise lists "Earliest Patient Sales (tentative): Spring 2027." — CannBus Q3 2026 Market Report — accessed 2026-10-30. No official Commission sales-start date was located.
  • Medical tax revenue: $0 from medical cannabis sales follows from zero sales; no official revenue figure exists. (Tax rates themselves belong to Part 2 and are not decided here.)

6.2 Projected market figures (secondary, dated, with their stale assumption flagged)

  • Vicente LLP / Nebraska Families 4 Medical Cannabis projection (pre-implementation; publication date not shown on the page as opened):
  • "Nebraska is projected to register over 56 thousand cannabis patients by the end of 2028 and over 93 thousand patients by the end of 2030." — Vicente LLP, press release, "Medical Cannabis Projected to Generate Over $925 Million in Economic Benefits for Nebraska Communities Through 2030" — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • "These patients are estimated to generate over $90 million in sales in 2028 and more than $152 million in sales in 2030. Through 2030, Nebraska is projected to sell $440 million in medical cannabis resulting in more than $925 million of new economic activity." — Vicente LLP, same press release — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Method as stated: "Projections for Nebraska's medical cannabis market incorporate patient enrollment from similar programs in Arkansas, Missouri, and South Dakota. When these population-adjusted registration rates are applied to Nebraska…" — Vicente LLP, same press release — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Stale-assumption flag (important): the projection is expressly conditioned on "If medical cannabis sales begin in mid-2026" — Vicente LLP, same press release — accessed 2026-10-30 — which did not happen (no dispensary licensed, earliest tentative sales spring 2027 per § 6.1). The 2028/2030 figures therefore cannot be quoted as current forecasts without re-basing; treat them as a dated campaign-era projection, Verify for any update.
  • No other dated Nebraska-specific market-size projection was located in the sources opened. In particular, no official (Commission, Department of Revenue or Legislature fiscal-note) market-size or revenue forecast was located — Unavailable / follow-up: check LB1235 fiscal note and any Commission fiscal estimate.

6.3 Hemp acreage and licence counts by year

Regime change (official): Nebraska no longer runs its own hemp licensing. "Persons interested in cultivating hemp in Nebraska may apply for a license from the USDA or a tribal program authorized under the Agriculture Improvement Act of 2018. Nebraska applicants may submit their hemp production application to the USDA starting in fall of 2024 to receive a USDA hemp producer license on January 1, 2025." — Nebraska Department of Agriculture, Hemp page, updated January 2025 — accessed 2026-10-30. The same page links the NDA annual summaries for 2019–2024. — Nebraska Department of Agriculture, Hemp page — accessed 2026-10-30.

A federal ban on hemp-derived THC products puts the crop in jeopardy" (2026) — accessed 2026-10-30. No revenue, volume or market-share figure for that company was given, and none is estimated here.

6.4 Nebraska residents buying in Colorado, Missouri and other states

Official figures first — none located that quantify Nebraska residents' out-of-state purchases.

  • No official figure (from Colorado, Missouri, Iowa, South Dakota or Nebraska state agencies) stating how many Nebraska residents bought cannabis out of state, or how much they spent, was located in the sources opened. Dollar or headcount estimates from any source would be invention and are not given.
  • Survey evidence (official university source, not a sales figure): The University of Nebraska–Lincoln Rural Drug Addiction Research Center's Nebraska Annual Social Indicators Survey found: "Eleven percent of Nebraskans surveyed said they used marijuana purchased legally out of state in 2024, while 15% said the same in 2026." — UNL / Newswise, "Report: Nebraskans' support of legal cannabis is growing," published 22 July 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30. The same report notes delta-8/other THC product use rose from 8% (2024) to 10% (2026), medical-cannabis support rose from 80% (2024) to 84% (2026), and support for legal marijuana for both medical and recreational use rose from 44% (2024) to 47% (2026). — UNL Research, same report — accessed 2026-10-30. These are survey percentages of respondents, not sales volumes, and the survey sample size/margin were not stated in the pages opened — Verify sample details before quoting precision.
  • Where Nebraskans can legally buy (context, not a Nebraska sales figure): Adults 21+ with valid government photo ID from any state can buy adult-use cannabis in Colorado and Missouri (among 23 such states listed), while Nebraska itself is listed among 17 states with no state-licensed store selling to visitors. — Alexis 420 / TravelPulse 50-state guide, 25–26 Oct 2026 (secondary directory) — accessed 2026-10-30. Secondary Omaha guidance puts the closest Missouri adult-use option for Omaha residents at Rock Port, MO, "roughly 70 to 75 driving miles away," and notes an Iowa medical dispensary in Council Bluffs, IA (about 1.5 miles from downtown Omaha) generally requires Iowa residency/card — distances are secondary estimates, Verify before routing anyone. — Herb.co, "How to Buy Weed in Omaha" (secondary city guide) — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Border-enforcement context (not a sales figure): In September 2026 the Nebraska Attorney General issued a demand/subpoena to Peak North Holdings, operator of Peak Dispensary in Sedgwick, Colorado (less than a half-hour drive from the Nebraska border), which petitioned a Nebraska court to resist it; the stated basis was not disclosed in the reporting. — MJBizDaily, "Nebraska attorney general probes Colorado cannabis company," 24 Sep 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30. This is included only as evidence that cross-border purchasing is a live enforcement topic, not as a volume estimate.
  • What can legally be brought back into Nebraska: covered in Part 10 (interstate), not decided here; no out-of-state purchase becomes legal in Nebraska by virtue of being legal where bought, and transport across state lines remains a federal issue per the fixed federal baseline in the commission.

6.5 Cannabis/hemp-friendly banks and credit unions

  • No Nebraska-chartered bank or Nebraska credit union publishing a cannabis/hemp banking programme was located. A 2019 Nebraska Bankers Association statement is the closest on-record Nebraska banking position found, and it points the other way: "At this point, our banking industry has avoided banking marijuana-related businesses," said NBA president/CEO Richard Baier, while noting Nebraska banks do business on Colorado's Front Range. — Omaha Daily Record, "Workforce, Cannabis Among Top Issues in Banking Industry" (2019) — accessed 2026-10-30. That statement is dated 2019 and pre-dates the medical programme; it is not current proof that no Nebraska bank serves hemp/CBD businesses today — Verify with the Nebraska Bankers Association / Nebraska Department of Banking and Finance.
  • One provider that publishes a Nebraska-specific cannabis service page (not a Nebraska bank): Safe Harbor Financial (SHF Holdings) publishes a "Nebraska Cannabis Banking and Financial Services" page stating "We work with cannabis related businesses in the state of Nebraska including dispensaries, cultivators, manufacturers, labs, distributors, and ancillary businesses," and offering banking, lending, payment, cash management, payroll, accounting and tax support. Its listed address on that page is Golden, Colorado — it is a Colorado-based fintech/platform working through partner financial institutions, not a Nebraska-chartered depository, and no Nebraska branch is stated. — Safe Harbor Financial, Nebraska page — accessed 2026-10-30. Include it only with that caveat; do not present it as a Nebraska bank.
  • Excluded on purpose: Credit Union 1, Community Choice Credit Union and Herring Bank all publish cannabis-banking pages, but none was shown to be Nebraska-based or Nebraska-branch-based in the pages returned, so they are not listed as Nebraska cannabis-friendly institutions. Their pages were returned in a general search and are not evidence of Nebraska availability. — Credit Union 1, Cannabis Business Banking — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Hemp banking: No Nebraska-specific hemp-banking programme publication was located; hemp businesses' banking difficulty was noted qualitatively in academic work on first-year Nebraska hemp farmers ("difficulty … securing insurance and loans" among the complexities reported). — Cambridge, Renewable Agriculture and Food Systems, "Challenges from the field: experiences of first-year hemp farmers in Nebraska" — accessed 2026-10-30. No bank is named there.

  • No recurring Nebraska cannabis/hemp trade show or expo was confirmed. Searches for Nebraska cannabis/hemp expos in Omaha/Lincoln returned no Nebraska event with a confirmed date/venue; the events returned were out of state (NoCo Hemp Expo, Denver; CannaCon Midwest, St. Paul/St. Louis; Lucky Leaf Expo, Minneapolis) or general business events, which are not Nebraska events and are excluded from the list below.

  • Good Life Fest 2026, Falconwood Park (Bellevue/Omaha area), 1 Aug 2026 — a music festival (20+ bands, 3 stages) at which the Omaha hemp/CBD retailer Lucid Dreams presented and operated a storefront on festival grounds. This is a music festival with a hemp-retail presence, not a cannabis trade show. — Lucid Dreams / LocalGymsAndFitness listing, "Lucid Dreams (2026)" — Good Life Fest 2026, 31 July–1 Aug 2026 posts — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify venue/date against the festival's own site before listing it as an industry event.

The October 2026 hearing is reported at the State Office Building, downtown Lincoln, with written comments due by 11:59 p.m. that Thursday. — Marijuana Moment, Oct 2026 — accessed 2026-10-30.

  • Conclusion for this section: As of 2026-10-30, Nebraska has no confirmed, recurring, Nebraska-based cannabis/hemp trade show to list. Do not import NoCo, CannaCon or MJBizCon (Las Vegas) into a Nebraska events list.

Part 7 — Hemp Licences

PART 7 — HEMP LICENCES

7.0 The plan answer first: Nebraska does NOT run its own USDA-approved state plan today

  • The USDA Agricultural Marketing Service list, updated 17 February 2026, lists Nebraska as “USDA Hemp Producer License,” and defines that descriptor as “USDA will issue the producer license.” Source (opened directly): https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • That is a change from history, not a contradiction: USDA announced approval of Nebraska's state plan in January 2020 (with Delaware and Texas and several tribal plans). Source (secondary, USDA announcement reporting): https://www.morningagclips.com/usda-approves-7-state-tribal-hemp-production-plans/ — accessed 2026-10-30. The state plan then ceased to be the licensing route when LB 262 (2024) transferred cultivation licensing to USDA from 1 January 2025 (see §7.3).
  • Tribal plans are separate and do not make Nebraska a state-plan state. The same AMS list (updated 17 February 2026) separately lists, among others, the Omaha Tribe of Nebraska, the Winnebago Tribe of Nebraska and the Santee Sioux Nation as “Approved Plan (pdf),” and the Iowa Tribe of Kansas and Nebraska as “Approved Plan (pdf).” Source: https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review — accessed 2026-10-30. A producer on tribal land is licensed under the applicable tribal plan, not under a Nebraska state plan; a producer elsewhere in Nebraska is USDA-licensed. Full tribal detail is Run 2, Part 10, not this part.
  • The Nebraska Department of Agriculture (NDA) hemp page, updated January 2025, states the current route in one paragraph: “Persons interested in cultivating hemp in Nebraska may apply for a license from the USDA or a tribal program authorized under the Agriculture Improvement Act of 2018. Nebraska applicants may submit their hemp production application to the USDA starting in fall of 2024 to receive a USDA hemp producer license on January 1, 2025.” Source (opened directly): https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp — accessed 2026-10-30. The same page links the NDA annual summaries for 2019–2024 only — there is no 2025 NDA summary, consistent with the programme ending.

7.1 Current USDA programme (from 1 January 2025) — one producer licence type

  • Licence type: USDA hemp producer licence only. There is no separate USDA “processor,” “handler” or “broker” licence in 7 CFR Part 990, Subpart C, as opened here. USDA's grower page states the route generally: “To produce hemp, you first need to be licensed or authorized under a state hemp program, a tribal hemp program, or the USDA hemp program,” and “If your state or tribe does not have a pending or approved hemp production plan, you may apply for a USDA hemp production license” by creating an account on the Hemp eManagement Platform (HeMP) and submitting a USDA Hemp Application, accepted “on a rolling basis throughout the year.” Source: https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/information-for-hemp-growers — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • What it allows: production of hemp (cultivation) in accordance with the USDA plan, at the locations listed on the licence. Verbatim, 7 CFR §990.21 (via govinfo text opened in search): “Any person producing or intending to produce hemp must have a valid license prior to producing hemp.” Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2022-title7-vol8/pdf/CFR-2022-title7-vol8-sec990-21.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Fee: none stated in the 7 CFR §§990.21–990.22 excerpts opened; secondary sources consistently report no USDA application or renewal fee — Verify the fee position on the current AMS application page before quoting “no fee” as an official fact, because the AMS grower page opened here does not itself state a fee. Secondary: https://hempcbdbusinessplans.com/nebraska-hemp-industry/ — accessed 2026-10-30 (reports “There is no application fee” and “There is no renewal application fee” for USDA licences generally).
  • Requirements (7 CFR §§990.21–990.22, opened via govinfo/Cornell text): application may be submitted at any time; the applicant must provide the information requested on the application form, including contact information and production-location information for each lot/greenhouse/location; a current criminal history report for the applicant and, for an entity, every key participant; and a licence will not be issued unless the application is complete and accurate, the criminal history reports confirm no key participant has a state or federal felony conviction relating to a controlled substance within the past 10 years (unless the exception in §990.20(b) applies — the scope of that exception was not opened in this track, Verify), the applicant has submitted all required reports if previously licensed, the application contains no materially false statements, and the applicant is not a stand-in for a suspended/revoked/ineligible person. Sources: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2022-title7-vol8/pdf/CFR-2022-title7-vol8-sec990-21.pdf and https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/7/990.22 — accessed 2026-10-30. Secondary detail, Verify against the HeMP application itself: the FBI criminal history report must be dated within 60 days of submission. Source (secondary): https://legalclarity.org/do-you-need-a-license-to-grow-hemp/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Duration, renewal, transfer: “Licenses will be valid until December 31 of the year three after the year in which the license was issued” and “Licenses may not be sold, assigned, transferred, pledged, or otherwise disposed of, alienated or encumbered.” Renewal must occur prior to expiration, with the same information as the initial application (secondary summary of §990.21; the renewal-information point is Verify against the current HeMP renewal form). A denied applicant may appeal under Subpart D of Part 990. Sources: https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/7/990.22 and https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2022-title7-vol8/pdf/CFR-2022-title7-vol8-sec990-21.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • No Nebraska processor-handler or broker licence exists to apply for today — and the gap is real. LB 262 outright repealed the former licensing sections, including §2-506 (processor-handler/broker) and §2-508 (fees) — see §7.3. The current Nebraska Hemp Farming Act, as opened (display of §§2-501–2-519), authorises cultivation by a USDA-licensed or tribal-program producer (§2-505) and transport (§2-515) but contains no processor, handler or broker licence. Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/display_html.php?begin_section=2-501&end_section=2-519 — accessed 2026-10-30. Verify before advising any processor: secondary NDA-era advice was to consult an attorney about the legality of processing/handling/brokering activities in 2025 after the NDA licences expired. Source (secondary): https://hempcbdbusinessplans.com/nebraska-hemp-industry/ — accessed 2026-10-30. This report does not fill that gap with an assumption. A USDA producer licence is a production licence; it is not, on the texts opened here, a downstream processing licence, and no Nebraska food/manufacturing licence specific to hemp was located in this track (general Pure Food Act applicability is a Run 1, Part 1 question, not decided here).
  • Registries (cross-reference Run 1, Part 5): current Nebraska producers are in USDA's Public Search Tool / HeMP (interactive search, no download confirmed in Run 1); historical NDA licensee counts (not names) are in the NDA annual summaries linked from https://nda.nebraska.gov/hemp — accessed 2026-10-30. No Nebraska-specific USDA producer count was published on the pages opened here — Unavailable without running the USDA Public Search Tool. Do not quote a 2025/2026 USDA Nebraska count from this track.

7.2 Former NDA programme (2019–2024, HISTORICAL — repealed, do not apply for these licences)

  • Legal basis: the Nebraska Hemp Farming Act, LB 657 (2019), as amended by LB 1152 (2020) and LB 263 (2023), administered by NDA under a USDA-approved state plan (approved January 2020). The Act's citation section, still on the books, reads: “Sections 2-501 to 2-518 shall be known and may be cited as the Nebraska Hemp Farming Act.” Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/display_html.php?begin_section=2-501&end_section=2-519 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Licence types (historical): (1) cultivator — to cultivate hemp (§2-505, historical text); (2) processor-handler — to process (convert hemp plants/parts into a marketable form) or handle (possess/store hemp plants or parts before, during or after harvest but before processing) (§2-506, historical text, and definitions in §2-503); (3) broker — to broker hemp plants or plant parts (§2-506, historical text). Verbatim, historical §2-506(1) (via Justia 2022 text): “Except for handling by an approved testing facility, a USDA-licensed hemp producer, or a cultivator licensed under section 2-505, a person shall not process, handle, or broker hemp plants or plant parts in this state unless the person meets the requirements of section 2-5701 or is in compliance with this section and licensed as a processor-handler or broker under the Nebraska Hemp Farming Act.” Source: https://law.justia.com/codes/nebraska/2022/chapter-2/statute-2-506/ — accessed 2026-10-30. All three NDA licence types expired 31 December 2024 and were not renewed (see §7.3).
  • Fees (historical, §2-508, LB 657 §8): fees were due on or before 31 December, set by the director on or before 1 July each year, not greater than column B. Verbatim fee table (via Justia 2022 text): application fee for cultivator, processor-handler and broker — column A $100, column B $150; cultivator site registration — $400 per site (A) / $600 per site (B); processor-handler site registration — $800 per site (A) / $1,200 per site (B); site modification fee — $50 (A) / $75 (B). Source: https://law.justia.com/codes/nebraska/2022/chapter-2/statute-2-508/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Secondary NDA-era summaries reporting the column-B (maximum) figures as the charged figures ($150 application, $600 cultivator site, $1,200 processor-handler site) are consistent with this table but the year-by-year director-set fee for each season is Verify against the relevant NDA annual summary. Broker site-registration fee: Verify — the §2-506 excerpt opened states a site fee for processing/handling sites; whether a broker (who need not hold a site) paid a site fee is not settled in the excerpts opened.
  • Requirements (historical): application on NDA-prescribed form including, for cultivators, proposed acreage or greenhouse/indoor square footage and certification that all sites were under the applicant's control with unlimited NDA access; site registration fee per separate site; site modifications only on NDA approval with the modification fee. Verbatim, historical §2-505(4) (via Justia 2022 text): “Before a person may be licensed to cultivate hemp under the Nebraska Hemp Farming Act, such person shall submit a site registration fee as set by the department pursuant to section 2-508. The site registration fee shall be paid for each separate site where the applicant will cultivate hemp.” Source: https://law.justia.com/codes/nebraska/2022/chapter-2/statute-2-505/ — accessed 2026-10-30. Initial processor-handler/broker applications could be submitted at any time and expired 31 December of the issue year; renewals were due 31 December for the following calendar year. A processor-handler or broker licence “shall lapse automatically upon a change of ownership or location, and a new license must be obtained,” with prompt notice to NDA. Source: https://law.justia.com/codes/nebraska/2022/chapter-2/statute-2-506/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Background checks and consent (historical, §2-510, LB 657 §10 as amended LB 1152 §6): every cultivator, processor-handler or broker consented to (a) a background check for any felony controlled-substance charge in the 10 years before application, at the applicant's expense, for all individuals listed on the application; (b) entry onto and inspection of all registered sites by NDA, with or without cause and with reasonable advance notice; (c) reimbursement of NDA sampling/testing expenses; (d) destruction of non-compliant, off-site or unlawfully handled hemp (see §7.4); and (e) at least annual NDA inspections. All financial-loss risk was the licensee's; no compensation was paid for destroyed hemp. Source: https://law.justia.com/codes/nebraska/2020/chapter-2/statute-2-510/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

7.3 How the state programme ended — LB 262 (2024)

  • LB 262, an omnibus Agriculture Committee bill, passed Final Reading 11 April 2024 on a 47–0 vote and was approved by the Governor 16 April 2024. Its hemp provisions (from LB 999, Sen. Ibach) transferred “licensure and regulation of hemp cultivation from the state Department of Agriculture to the United States Department of Agriculture, beginning Jan. 1, 2025,” terminated the state Hemp Commission and the Hemp Promotion Fund (money to the Noxious Weed Cash Fund), and required the state to continue regulating hemp transportation. Source (Unicameral Update, official Legislature news): http://update.legislature.ne.gov/?p=36361 — accessed 2026-10-30. Final-reading text: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/FloorDocs/108/PDF/Final/LB262.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Operative effect, as shown on the current statute display: §§2-502, 2-504, 2-506, 2-507, 2-508, 2-510, 2-511, 2-512, 2-513, 2-514, 2-516, 2-517, 2-519 and 2-5701 are marked “Repealed. Laws 2024, LB262, § 52,” and the Hemp Program Fund “terminates on January 1, 2025.” Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/display_html.php?begin_section=2-501&end_section=2-519 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Consequence, stated precisely: from 1 January 2025 there is no NDA cultivator, processor-handler or broker licence to hold, renew or apply for. The surviving Nebraska hemp statutes that matter for licensing are §2-503 (definitions), §2-505 (cultivation only by a USDA-licensed or tribal-program producer; transport only per §2-515) and §2-515 (transport documentation). Verbatim, current §2-505(1): “Hemp may be cultivated in this state by a USDA-licensed hemp producer, in accordance with such producer's USDA-issued license, or by a state-program-licensed hemp producer, in accordance with such producer's license under a USDA-approved tribal program.” Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/display_html.php?begin_section=2-501&end_section=2-519 — accessed 2026-10-30.

7.4 Hemp plot registration, pre-harvest sampling, testing and destruction

Former NDA system (historical): - Plot/site registration was the licence itself: each separate cultivation, processing or handling site had to be listed, with legal description/GPS coordinates, and a site fee paid per site; unlisted locations were not covered, and hemp at an unlisted location was subject to destruction (§2-510, above). - Sampling/testing: LB 263 (2023) aligned state law with the USDA Final Rule — it removed the statutory prescription that sampling occur at each lot (deferring to the hemp plan, which continued to require lot sampling unless amended), increased the harvest deadline after sample collection from 15 to 30 days, required 7 days' notice of a request for official sample collection (licensee bearing risk of damage from failure to timely request sampling), and added remediation if allowed by NDA. It also raised the negligent-violation threshold from 0.5% to 1.0% THC — expressly not raising the 0.3% harvestable limit: “Any hemp above 0.3% THC is still subject to destruction.” Source (LB 263 committee statement, official Legislature): https://www.nebraskalegislature.gov/FloorDocs/108/PDF/CS/LB263.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. - Destruction (historical §2-510): NDA could require destruction of (i) hemp from lots with measured delta-9 THC above the acceptable hemp THC level (only those lots), (ii) hemp for commercial purposes at a location not in the application, and (iii) hemp cultivated/processed/handled/brokered in violation of the Act or its rules. LB 263's summary states destruction was to be performed within 30 days of NDA notification — Verify the 30-day destruction deadline against the LB 263 enrolled text, as the committee summary is the source opened here. Source: https://www.nebraskalegislature.gov/FloorDocs/108/PDF/CS/LB263.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30.

Current USDA system (from 1 January 2025): - Plot registration is by licence application plus acreage reporting: the licence application must give production information for each location/lot/greenhouse (7 CFR §990.21, above). The Farm Service Agency (FSA) acreage-report requirement (report within 30 days of planting, with street address, geospatial location, acreage/square footage and licence number) is reported in secondary USDA summaries citing 7 CFR §990.23 — Verify the 30-day FSA deadline against the current §990.23 text, which was not opened directly in this track. Source (secondary): https://legalclarity.org/can-i-legally-grow-hemp-in-my-backyard/ — accessed 2026-10-30. - Pre-harvest sampling: a sampling agent must collect samples no more than 30 days before the anticipated harvest; the producer must harvest no later than 30 days after sample collection, or a second pre-harvest sample is required; harvested lots must not be commingled. Testing is for total THC (delta-9 THC + THCA × 0.877), reported on a dry-weight basis with measurement of uncertainty. Sources (7 CFR §§990.24–990.26 via govinfo): https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2022-title7-vol8/pdf/CFR-2022-title7-vol8-sec990-25.pdf and https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2022-title7-vol8/pdf/CFR-2022-title7-vol8-sec990-28.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. Sampling must be by a USDA- or programme-authorised sampling agent; testing laboratories must be DEA-registered, subject to the fixed-baseline enforcement delay to 31 December 2026 (an AMS FAQ snippet still showing a 31 December 2025 deadline is stale against that baseline — Verify the current AMS enforcement notice before quoting a DEA-lab date). - Non-compliance, remediation and destruction (7 CFR §§990.27–990.29, via govinfo): cannabis exceeding the acceptable hemp THC level is non-compliant and must be disposed of or remediated; a producer electing remediation must re-sample and re-test the post-remediation crop; a test result above the acceptable level is conclusive evidence the lot is non-compliant. A negligent violation is a separate tier: hemp above 0.3% but not above 1.0% total THC is subject to remediation/disposal but is not a negligent violation; above 1.0% is a negligent violation, triggering a corrective action plan (at least two years); no more than one negligent violation per calendar year; a producer violating a licence three times in a five-year period has the licence revoked and is ineligible to produce hemp for five years; a culpable mental state greater than negligence is referred/enforced separately. Sources: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CFR-2022-title7-vol8/pdf/CFR-2022-title7-vol8-sec990-28.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. Disposal methods (plowing, disking, composting, burial, burning, DEA reverse distributor, law enforcement) are stated in USDA guidance summaries — Verify the current USDA remediation/disposal guidance list before quoting a method, as the govinfo excerpts opened here state the duty, not the method list. - Nebraska's response to the future federal hemp dates: as of 30 Oct 2026, no Nebraska statute, NDA guidance or Commission rule located in this track changes the state hemp definition in anticipation of PL 119-37 §781 (12 Nov 2026) / PL 119-103 §2019 (11 Dec 2026). The state definition in §2-503 remains delta-9 THC “of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis” (as the section existed 1 January 2024), and the USDA total-THC test already applies to USDA producers. The post-11 December 2026 federal total-THC definition and 0.4 mg per-container cap are therefore future federal law for Nebraska producers, not current Nebraska law on the research date — Verify against any NDA/USDA guidance issued after 30 Oct 2026 before republication. A UNL survey report (22 July 2026, secondary/official university) notes a law redefining THC thresholds “set to go into effect in November” — that is the federal change, not a Nebraska enactment. Source: https://research.unl.edu/blog/report-nebraskans-support-of-legal-cannabis-is-growing/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

7.5 Hemp transport (the one state function LB 262 expressly retained)

  • Verbatim, current §2-515(1) (opened directly): “Except as provided in subsection (3) of this section, any USDA-licensed hemp producer or state-program-licensed hemp producer transporting hemp shall carry with the hemp being transported a copy of the USDA license or state program license under which it was cultivated and a copy of the test results pertaining to each lot of hemp being transported.” Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=2-515 — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Exceptions (§2-515(3)): test results need not be carried if the producer carries the licence and is transporting (a) hemp between two registered sites on the producer's licence application, (b) samples for THC testing, or (c) live plants to a registered site before cultivation. Tribal-program producers must keep shipment records (date, origin, destination, sender, receiver, vehicle identification number) (§2-515(2)). A non-producer transporter must carry a bill of lading (owner, origin, destination), a copy of the cultivation licence and lot test results, and must not unload except as authorised by state or federal law enforcement (§2-515(4)). No person may transport hemp concurrently with any other plant material that is not hemp (§2-515(5)). Source: https://nebraskalegislature.gov/laws/statutes.php?statute=2-515 — accessed 2026-10-30.

8.3 Known licensed-cultivator sites from Run 1 — context only, NOT Part 8 listings

No public sale/lease listing was found for any of these sites, and no street address for a cultivation site was established by a listing or zoning record opened in this run. They are therefore listed here only as Run 1 context, as instructed, and are not counted in §8.0.

  • MahāMotā Cultivation Company — Raymond, Nebraska. Run 1 baseline: the only licensed cultivator with plants in the ground; the Nebraska Medical Cannabis Commission voted to ratify its successful inspection, clearing the first legal plants. No street address for the Raymond cultivation site was stated in any source opened in this run, and no sale/lease listing for the site was found. It is not a Part 8 listing. Source for the Raymond location and inspection clearance: https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Midwest Cultivators Group (Midwest Cultivator Group) — Omaha → Gretna, Nebraska. Run 1 baseline: licensed cultivator approved to relocate from Omaha to Gretna after Omaha zoning changes. The Commission's 22 June 2026 draft minutes record that the group applied for a conditional use permit at its original Omaha location, was advised the answer would ultimately be no there, located a new address "right outside of Omaha in Gretna", and that the Gretna planning committee voted 28 April 2026 and the Gretna City Council voted 19 May 2026, both unanimously, to approve the conditional use permit; the Commission approved the address change. The street address of the new Gretna site is not stated in the minutes excerpt returned, and no sale/lease listing for it was found. It is therefore not a Part 8 listing, and no Gretna street address is asserted here. Source: https://mcc.nebraska.gov/sites/default/files/June%2022%20Draft%20Meeting%20Minutes%20changes.pdf — accessed 2026-10-30. Corroboration of the relocation and CUP approval: https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • KRL Med LLC — near Herman, Washington County, Nebraska (disputed site). Run 1 baseline: licensed cultivator stalled by a Washington County zoning dispute / stop-work order, with the county administrator reversing an agricultural exemption (hemp would qualify, marijuana would not) and a Commission licence renewal of six months. No street address and no sale/lease listing for the site was found in this run. It is not a Part 8 listing. Source: https://northplattepost.com/posts/134d6f2b-785d-4ba2-bc07-12c0af590b2c — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Meadowlark Medicinals LLC — site unavailable. Run 1 baseline: fourth licensed cultivator; its site was unavailable in Run 1 and remains unavailable in this run. No address and no listing was found. It is not a Part 8 listing. Source: Run 1 baseline above — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • No dispensary or manufacturer site can be listed, because no dispensary or manufacturer licence has been issued (Run 1 baseline). Any future dispensary site would also have to satisfy the state 1,000-foot buffer from covered locations (school, licensed childcare, church, hospital, mental-health / substance-use treatment centre) and the one-per-judicial-district cap, and, in Omaha, Omaha Ord. 44411 (dispensaries in general retail districts, no residential operation, no on-site consumption, hours 8 a.m.–11 p.m.) — but those zoning rules attach to no currently listed parcel in this run. Source for the Omaha zoning summary: https://nebraskapublicmedia.org/en/news/news-articles/city-of-omaha-approves-zoning-regulations-for-medical-cannabis-businesses/ — accessed 2026-10-30.

8.4 Checked and excluded — not counted, with reasons

  • O'Neill, Nebraska — Power REIT "MillPro" greenhouse (Holt County). A 1,121,513-square-foot greenhouse on about 86 acres, acquired 31 March 2022 for $9,350,000, configured for tomato cultivation, whose tenant defaulted and vacated during 2022; Power REIT later explored a new tenant or a sale. It is not a cannabis-zoned or cannabis-marketed Nebraska listing: its stated use was tomatoes, its cannabis connection is only that its owner holds cannabis greenhouses in other states and that greenhouses are generically convertible. No current Nebraska cannabis listing, price or cannabis tenant for the O'Neill site was located. Excluded. Sources: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1532619/000149315224012040/form10-k.htm — accessed 2026-10-30; https://www.costar.com/article/1963236002/slowing-cannabis-demand-threatens-greenhouse-owners-future — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Culbertson, Nebraska — Nebraska Industrial Hemp Processing plant (Hitchcock County). A news report describes a hemp processing plant north of Culbertson, built by a local farmer, as the only facility of its kind in Nebraska. It is a news report about an operating business, not a property listing, and it gives no asking price and no sale/lease status. Excluded. Source: https://gray-wowt-prod.gtv-cdn.com/2026/08/25/nebraskas-first-industrial-hemp-processing-plant-takes-root-culbertson/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • Huskerland Hemp, Beatrice, Nebraska (Penn-GARM LLC). A hemp business website selling hemp oil, balm, edibles and soap from a family farm. It is a business website, not a property listing, and it states no property for sale or lease, no price and no zoning. Excluded. Source: https://www.huskerlandhemp.com/ — accessed 2026-10-30.
  • General Nebraska commercial, warehouse, farm and ranch listings (LoopNet, Crexi, Land.com / LandWatch / Land & Farm). Ordinary listings with no cannabis or hemp use stated in their text or zoning were not counted, per the task rule against presenting a general listing as cannabis-zoned. See §8.2.2–§8.2.4.
  • Out-of-state traps excluded: Winona, MN (LoopNet cannabis micro-business, §8.2.2); Tampa, FL on N. Nebraska Avenue (§8.2.5); Long Beach / Humboldt County, CA, New Jersey, Washington, Michigan, Oklahoma and other states shown as "listings you may like" on 420 Property Nebraska and city pages (§8.2.1); and a Kentucky hemp greenhouse sale. None is in Nebraska and none is counted. South Sioux City, NE was kept distinct from Sioux City, IA, and Omaha, NE from Council Bluffs, IA, throughout: no listing was attributed across either state line.
  • Same-name places: no listing was accepted on a town name alone. Every counted or excluded property above was checked for its state (NE) and county/address before inclusion; in particular, no Lincoln, Kearney, Grand Island, Columbus, Norfolk, Fremont, Hastings, Bellevue, York, Lexington, Alliance or Sidney listing from another state was counted.
